APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Friday 6-Nov, on the 5-Nov close

Readings as at Thu 5-Nov-2026, Thursday's close (the dashboard data built at 06:13 UK on 6-Nov). The run started at about 12:15 UK on Friday, before Friday's close, so Thursday's close is the latest. The previous note read Tue 3-Nov; this one adds two sessions (4 and 5-Nov), in which the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% and then fell 0.4%, Technology fell 2.1% and Defence 4.1% over the same two sessions. The rules are your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026. The next reading is TODAY, Fri 6-Nov (Industry RS below 30; the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month), and its close is not in these readings; the next month-end reading is Mon 30-Nov. The next Tuesday session is 10-Nov.

The three things that matter most this week

1. Today's Friday reading can add Volex (5.73% of capital) and Sdiptech (7.49%) to the Tue 10-Nov sales, because Industry RS for Industrials and capital goods fell from 46 at the 30-Oct month-end to 26 on both 4 and 5-Nov, below the line of 30.

  • Why it matters: both are Middle Innings Core Investments outside the one-off in/out link, so an Industry RS reading below 30 today sells each whole holding at Tue 10-Nov. With Arteche and Alfen already due, the 10-Nov sales would come to about 20.1% of capital, all in Industrials and capital goods.
  • How close it is: Industry RS is a rank among the 16 industries; the next step above 26 is 33. The Sdiptech section's replica of the ranking puts Industrials and capital goods about 0.6 of a percentage point behind Utilities for that place, a gap it has closed on about one session in four over the past year. I grade both sales Probable.
  • Against the sale, for your ruling: Sdiptech's own readings are clear (Stock RS 88, a 13-month high); Volex's estimates are still rising and its interims fall on Thu 12-Nov, two sessions after the sale. The rule reads the industry, not the stock.

2. Two sales stand for Tue 10-Nov already: Arteche, the whole holding (3.74%), and Alfen, both remaining tranches (3.10%).

  • Arteche: relative price more than 25% below its 52W high at the 30-Oct month-end; the data file still dates the sale "Tuesday 2026-11-03", so the sale was not made or not recorded. The 5-Nov close of €27.7 broke below the 29-Jul and 1-Sep lows on 1.61 times its 50D average volume.
  • Alfen: exit confirmed on 28-Sep under the 10-day stop-loss rule (three consecutive closes at or below the 150D MA in the bottom half of the day's range), not sold; the stop-loss is €12.02 and the 5-Nov close €10.09. Alfen's SP rose 6.9% intraday on the 4-Nov Q3 results, closed up only 1.2% on 2.67 times average volume, then fell 4.4% on 5-Nov.

3. The two results of Thursday 5-Nov moved the case more than the price: Helios Towers broadened its case, IMCD's sell-side upgraded, and both sold off from the day's high.

  • Helios Towers (7.13%): third-quarter tenancy additions of about 924, above the 900 "raise" line of the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 3-Nov, and an FY2027 outlook of more than 3,000 additions; but Stock RS fell from 70 to 64, so both halves of "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" are present ahead of the 30-Nov reading.
  • IMCD (3.71%): FY2026 EPS +0.8% and the average PT +1.1% on 5-Nov, Net Upgrade Breadth positive on every line; the SP reversed from €105.2 to €103.35 on 1.70 times its 50D average volume, and €103.35 is 1.1% above tranche 1's 20D MA.

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the fortnight and the month. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the graded add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% on Wednesday 4-Nov and fell 0.4% on Thursday 5-Nov, which leaves it up 0.9% in the week, 2.7% over the fortnight and 3.9% over the month; over three months it is down 0.8%, over twelve months up 14.5%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 29-Oct close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 22-Oct close and the month the 21 sessions from the 7-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases; the industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months).

  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 5-Nov): Consumer staples +10.7%, Healthcare +9.3%, Professional, business and consumer services +8.3%, Media +8.1%, Consumer discretionary +7.6% and Real assets +6.2%.
  • The month's laggards: Technology (4.0%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.5%), Industrials and capital goods (1.1%), Transportation +0.3% and Materials +2.6%.
  • The week: Media +6.9%, Consumer staples +4.6% and Consumer discretionary +3.7% led; Defence (5.6%), Technology (5.3%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (3.3%), Transportation (2.3%) and Industrials and capital goods (1.5%) fell.
  • Thursday 5-Nov: Defence (3.2%), Technology (2.3%), Transportation (1.0%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.9%) led the fall; Media +1.3% and Telecoms +0.5% rose.
  • Over three months the split is wider: Healthcare +8.8%, Transportation +7.2% and Consumer staples +6.9%; Defence (11.6%), Real assets (5.1%) and Materials (2.1%).

The defensive rotation resumed this week after pausing in the last: Consumer staples, which rose only 1.0% in the week to 3-Nov, rose 4.6% in the week to 5-Nov and leads the month at +10.7%, with Healthcare second at +9.3%. Carlsberg made new 52W highs on 4 and 5-Nov with Beverages' leaders (Heineken and AB InBev), though not the whole sector (Coca-Cola HBC fell 17.9% on 5-Nov); Hilton Food made its highest close since Sep-2025 on 5-Nov. Fevertree is the exception, +1.6% in the week against Consumer staples' +4.6%. What would confirm the rotation: Consumer staples and Healthcare holding their month's gains through today's Friday reading while Technology and Industrials and capital goods keep falling; a reversal of the 4-Nov rally led by Technology would say the rotation is exhausted.

The AI and data-centre complex sold off on 4 and 5-Nov, and it carries three holdings: Volex and Arteche in Industrials and capital goods, Hexatronic in Technology. Technology fell 5.3% in the week and Industrials and capital goods 1.5%; inside them Volex fell 11.2% in the week with the AI data-centre peers (a seven-peer average of (8.1%)), Arteche fell 12.9% with Siemens Energy, Prysmian and ABB, and Hexatronic fell 19.5% with Corning and Prysmian. The sources read name no company news for Volex or Hexatronic on those days. What would confirm a sector-wide de-rating rather than a two-day shake-out: a second week of Technology falling on heavy volume; a heavy-volume up day in Prysmian and Corning would point the other way.

Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry (about 23.8% of capital on current values: Sdiptech 7.49%, Volex 5.73%, Arteche 3.74%, IMCD 3.71%, Alfen 3.10%), now reads Industry RS 26, from 46 at the 30-Oct month-end and 79 on 2-Oct. Below 30 at today's reading sells Volex and Sdiptech in full at Tue 10-Nov; the Opening and the Volex and Sdiptech sections give the odds.

Defence fell 4.1% in two sessions after Rheinmetall's 2026 guidance cut on 4-Nov (Euronews, 5-Nov), and its Industry RS is 0. Theon fell 10.5% in the week, about twice Defence's 5.6%. The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting table moved Defence to Underweight on 30-Oct on the Defence de-rating; this week's fall is consistent with it.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the table of 30-Oct-2026, the newest; a new table may follow today's close): Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Defence and Media; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included; Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight (risk-off beneath a steady index, led by credit and the cost of debt). Its logic, in brief: own the defensives with rising estimates, avoid the leveraged and rate-sensitive, and keep cyclical growth only where its own estimates rise. The book is the other way round on size: Consumer staples is about 11.9% of capital (Carlsberg, Fevertree, Hilton Food), against about 23.8% in Industrials and capital goods. This week's moves rewarded the table only in part: its Overweight Consumer staples rose 4.6% and its Underweight Defence fell 5.6%, against +0.9% for the Stoxx Europe 600; but its Underweight Media led the week (+6.9%), its Double Underweight Consumer discretionary (+3.7%) and Real assets (+3.5%) beat the index, and its other Overweight, Energy, commodities and metals mining, fell 3.3%.

Your 3-Nov market note, answered where the new data can answer it (the note is unchanged since the previous run, which answered it in full).

  • On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Staples RS really accelerating but still a very low P/E." The week (+4.6%) and the month (+10.7%) confirm the acceleration, after the pause of the week to 3-Nov. Valuation is outside this note.
  • On 3-Nov you wrote: "Healthcare RS absolutely ripped since Sep-26." Healthcare is +9.3% over the month and +8.8% over three months, the best industry over three months. Xvivo is not part of it: its Sector RS rose from 67 to 88 in four sessions while its Stock RS is 8.
  • On 3-Nov you wrote: "ES600 still in a decent uptrend IMHO". The index is +14.5% over twelve months and +3.9% over the month, but (0.8%) over three months: the uptrend has been flat since August.

Arteche

Middle Innings Core Investment · 3.74% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 1.63%, bought 26-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.23%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 0.88%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Thu 5-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Arteche's SP broke below both lows of its summer base on 5-Nov: it fell 4.5% to €27.7, below the €27.8 close of 29-Jul (where the September advance began) and the €28.0 close of 1-Sep, to its lowest close since 14-Apr-2026. Arteche is down 12.9% in five sessions (from €31.8 on 29-Oct), its largest five-session fall in 12 months, and 26.5% from its €37.7 close of 30-Sep. Over 3 months it is down 2.8% and over 12 months up 37.2%; it is 37.0% below its €44.0 52W closing high (8-May-2026).

The legs since the 30-Sep high, against the industry (Industrials and capital goods, Market chart) and the large grid and electrification names (chart files): the falls of (5.8%) on 1-Oct and (5.2%) on 2-Nov were Arteche's own (the industry moved (1.3%) on 1-Oct and +1.0% on 2-Nov); the falls of (17.5%) from 6 to 23-Oct and (3.8%) on 30-Oct were the sector's; 4-Nov, (1.0%) to €29.0, the sector's, and Arteche held better (Stoxx Europe 600 +1.8%, Siemens Energy (8.0%), Prysmian (6.9%)); 5-Nov, (4.5%) to €27.7, the sector's (industry (0.9%), Siemens Energy (4.7%), ABB (4.2%)).

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run (the sign-in failed); the newest one read, Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026, found "no new material news flow" and no broker notes or filings for Arteche in its week. No Arteche announcement dated 4 or 5-Nov is in the sources read; the cause of Arteche's own falls on 1-Oct and 2-Nov is still not in them. Two dated items bear on the window:

  • The sector sell-off of 4 and 5-Nov: an ad-hoc-news report (6-Nov-2026) ties Siemens Energy's fall to an AI-equity sell-off and fears of tighter data-centre rules; item 9 (Peers) gives its wording and date detail.
  • The Strategic Plan 2027-2029, dated 30-Nov by the company's investor agenda (questions filed for the 5-Nov investor relations call, whose answers are not in the sources read): the day after Spain's 29-Nov election and the month-end reading day.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Arteche SP chart very negative L3D. Why is that. Something is happening. Down 15% in 2D. Up 2% today. Why such a big decline". The previous run's note (on the 3-Nov close, published 4-Nov) answered it (a fall of 8.8% in two sessions on closes; the cause of 2-Nov is not in the sources read). Since then the theme has turned against the whole sector. On 28-Oct you wrote "APM to confirm the successful retest of the rising 200D MA": the retest failed. Arteche closed 6.1% below the absolute 200D MA on 2-Nov and is 10.6% below it (€30.99) on 5-Nov.

The MAs: Arteche lost its 20D MA on 8-Oct, its 100D and 150D on 19-Oct and its 50D and absolute 200D on 22-Oct; it regained the absolute 200D MA on 27-Oct and lost it again on 30-Oct. The 20D and 100D MAs are falling; the 50D (€32.29, flat over 5 sessions), 150D and absolute 200D MAs are rising. Against your 5% principle (your Carlsberg note, 28 to 31-Oct: a price that holds "< 5% below an MA" "is holding/testing"), Arteche's 10.6% distance below the absolute 200D MA is twice that.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Arteche against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+3.2% over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€33.10 against €30.99).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: partly (above the absolute 200D MA only; €32.29 against €33.10).
  • The SP above each MA: fails (14.2% below the 50D, 16.3% below the 150D, 10.6% below the absolute 200D MA).
  • Near its 52W high: fails (37.0% below).
  • Away from its 52W low: passes, narrowing (39.9% above the €19.8 closing low of 18-Nov-2025).

On 2-Nov you asked: "APM says "seven Stage 3 flags present or partly present" - what are they". Here they are, counted from the 29-Jul low, at the 5-Nov close:

  • The largest one-day fall since the advance began: present (1-Oct, (5.8%) on 1.94x volume).
  • The largest one-week fall: present, and larger since 3-Nov (the (12.9%) five-session fall to 5-Nov described in this item).
  • Wider swings: present (average daily range 5.8% of the SP in the last five sessions, against 3.1% in August and 4.2% in September).
  • Lower lows: present (€27.7 below the 29-Jul and 1-Sep lows).
  • More down days than up days: present (16 against 10 since 30-Sep).
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly (the 30-Sep high on 0.76x, reversed on 1-Oct on 1.94x).
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: resolved downward (the absolute 200D MA, lost on 30-Oct).

Verdict: a broken trend, confirmed on 5-Nov. The previous run's note (on the 3-Nov close, published 4-Nov) set the settling test as "a close below €28.0 would confirm the break", and the 5-Nov close (€27.7) met it on heavy volume. What would change it: a heavy-volume close back above the absolute 200D MA (€30.99, 11.9% above the close); short of that, a heavy-volume up day closing above €29.0 would be the first sign of a low.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the two mechanical-volume days in Arteche's 50D average (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review: about 24,800 shares without them, 31,900 with), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets.

Arteche's 5-Nov fall to a new low came on the heaviest volume of the whole decline from 30-Sep: sellers are now arriving at the lows, the opposite of a decline drying up. On 5-Nov Arteche fell 4.5% on 40,032 shares (€1.11m), 1.61x (1.26x) its 50D average. It opened 1.4% higher, traded up to €29.8 and closed at 0.25 of the day's range: a gap up sold into. On 4-Nov Arteche fell 1.0% on 0.85x (0.66x), closing at the day's low.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: one, 5-Nov; the 1-Oct day (1.94x) left the window on 5-Nov, so the one heavy day is now at the low, not the top.

Distribution days on your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average or more, in the last 25 sessions): two without the mechanical-volume days, 2-Nov (1.41x) and 5-Nov (1.61x), both in the last four sessions. On the dashboard's own basis 5-Nov qualifies too (1.26x), yet the dashboard counts none in 25 sessions (a data fault). No accumulation day has come in the 25 sessions on either basis.

Up-day volume as a share of down-day volume (average shares on up days divided by average shares on down days): 0.69 over 5 sessions (the dashboard's figure), 0.83 over 20, and 0.78 over the whole decline from 30-Sep (10 up days averaging 18,700 shares, 16 down days 23,900), against 0.87 in the May to July 2026 decline (both without mechanical-volume days): buyers are thinner in this decline than in the last.

Volume through the move rose at the end: Arteche's average daily volume fell from 32,900 shares in the first five sessions of October (1 to 7-Oct) to 13,500 in the week to 30-Oct, then doubled to 29,100 from 2 to 5-Nov (5-session average 1.05x the 50D average without mechanical-volume days, 0.82x on the dashboard's).

Volume at the MAs: Arteche broke its 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 22-Oct on 0.79x (0.61x) and lost the absolute 200D MA again on 30-Oct on 0.60x (0.46x); the moves away from it came on 1.41x (2-Nov) and 1.61x (5-Nov).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I read the volume charts L3M positively - why does this clash with the SP trends + the APM comments". Your reading was right for the selling and missed the buying. From 12 to 30-Oct Arteche's selling was light (13,500 to 18,400 shares a day), and the dashboard's 50D average, lifted by the 18-Sep index review, made it look about 20% lighter still. But no up day in the decline reached 1.25x, and since 2-Nov the selling has turned heavy too.

Precedent: how Arteche's 2026 declines ended. The June to July decline ended with a 6.1% up day on 2.09x the day after the 29-Jul low, closing at its high; the May to June decline ended quietly and its light rebound failed. The 5-Nov session is neither: it is a heavy-volume down day through both earlier lows (€27.8 on 29-Jul and €28.0 on 1-Sep), with no reversal yet.

Arteche's relative price is 41.8% below its 52W high (12-May-2026) and 15.7% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. It was 34.7% below at the 30-Oct month-end reading and 37.7% on 3-Nov. Relative price confirmed the SP's new low by closing at its lowest since 9-Mar-2026, as it had confirmed the SP's May high.

Peers' volume makes 4 and 5-Nov the sector's selling days, and 5-Nov the day Arteche joined: on 4-Nov Siemens Energy, Prysmian, Legrand and Schneider Electric fell 3.4% to 8.0% on 1.84x to 3.17x their 50D averages; on 5-Nov Siemens Energy, ABB, Prysmian and Alfen fell 4.0% to 4.7% on 1.70x to 2.82x, alongside Arteche (both without mechanical-volume days).

Confirms a turn: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 37,300 shares, €1.03m at €27.7), closing in the top third of its range and back above €29.0 (the 2-Nov and 4-Nov closes). Confirms the decline continues: a further close below €27.0 (the 5-Nov intraday low) on more than 24,800 shares.

3. RS

All three of Arteche's RS readings fell to 13-month lows on 4-Nov, together, for the first time in this decline: Stock RS 45, Sector RS 5, Industry RS 26.

  • Stock RS: 94 (2-Oct), 83 (23-Oct), 76 (30-Oct), 63 (3-Nov), 51 (4-Nov), 45 (5-Nov): (49) points in a month and (18) in two sessions; below the line of 50 that the test of Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS reads at the month-end, the lowest in the data file's 13 months (before November the low was 73, on 11-Sep).
  • Sector RS (Electrical equipment, grid and automation): 75 (2-Oct), 13 (23-Oct), 25 (30-Oct), 24 (3-Nov), 4 (4-Nov), 5 (5-Nov): below 50 since 16-Oct.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 (9-Oct), 46 (23 and 30-Oct, 3-Nov), 26 (4 and 5-Nov): below the 30 line the Friday test reads, and below its 12-month low of 40 (March 2026).

The order of the turn: sector first (9 to 16-Oct), industry second (16 to 23-Oct), Arteche last (late October), and on 4-Nov all three at once. The 4-Nov move was a rotation out of Industrials and capital goods: the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% that day and the industry fell 0.3% (Market chart). Late October's fall in Stock RS was Arteche's own; 4 and 5-Nov's was the industry's and sector's carrying Arteche.

Against peers, Arteche's Stock RS has now fallen as far as the large names' did, but from higher: Siemens Energy 6 (57 a month ago), Schneider Electric 11 (47), Prysmian 15 (78), Legrand 20 (56), ABB 22 (76). Only Landis+Gyr (72, from 53), Cenergy (61, from 95) and Siemens (48, from 64) stand above Arteche among the sector names the PMS Per-stock Dashboard shows.

Precedent: the last time Arteche's Stock RS stood near 70 (73, 11-Sep), Spain's €17bn grid plan (La Moncloa, 16-Sep) took it back to 96 in two weeks; no Spanish catalyst of that kind is dated before the 29-Nov election. As November 2025's low closes roll out of the 12-month base, Stock RS also faces a mechanical drag into December. Test: the Fri 6-Nov Industry RS reading (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion)).

4. Consensus by named FY

Arteche's consensus has not moved for five weeks: the SP has fallen 26.5% from its 30-Sep high on flat estimates. FY2026 EPS (€1.077), FY2027 EPS (€1.333) and FY2028 EPS (€1.56) are unchanged since 30-Sep, with three analysts on each; revenue and EBITDA are also unchanged over one month.

  • Over 3 months the nearer years' EPS rose fastest: FY2026 +9.3%, FY2027 +5.4%, FY2028 +1.3%. Over 6 months FY2026 and FY2027 EPS rose 17.5% and 17.6% while FY2028 EPS fell 1.3% and FY2028 EBITDA 1.0%: analysts pulled earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory.
  • Implied growth and margin: EPS +23.8% from FY2026 to FY2027 and +17.0% to FY2028, on revenue growth of 16.6% and 14.9%; the implied EBITDA margin rises from 17.7% (FY2026) to 18.5% and 18.9%. FY2027 EPS consensus rose 34% in 12 months against 7% for FY2027 revenue: the upgrades were margin-led.

In your 28 to 31-Oct note you wrote: "ISSUE — Why are FY 2028 EPS estimates falling? MAJOR ISSUE. Ditto EBITDA. It's a 2028-specific issue (2027 is clean)". On the latest readings FY2028 is no longer falling: FY2028 EPS is +1.3% and FY2028 EBITDA +10.8% over 3 months; the fall is a 6-month reading (EPS (1.3%), EBITDA (1.0%), from the 30-Apr month-end).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion market note of 3-Nov: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Arteche passes that rule over 3 months for all three FYs and over 6 months for FY2026 and FY2027, fails it over 6 months for FY2028, and has had no upgrade at all for five weeks.

The estimates Arteche's SP is discounting are cuts. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) rates cuts of 5% or more to FY2027 and FY2028 consensus at about 60% and 65%, and puts a 70% chance on the first FY2027 EBITDA range (about €112m to €120m) landing below the €126.2m consensus. FY2026 consensus EBITDA (€103.7m) already sits 3.3% above the top of Arteche's raised guidance (€100.4m, 28-Jul; earnings review). Hypothesis: the SP is leading those cuts, as it led the June 2026 cut (FY2027 EPS (2.7%), one of two analysts cutting). Against it: no analyst has cut, and the 4 and 5-Nov falls were the sector's (item 1 (SP and MAs)). Test: the first FY2027 change, most likely after the Strategic Plan 2027-2029, which the company's investor agenda dates 30-Nov.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst has cut anything on Arteche: 3 Buys of 3, and EPS, EBITDA, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth all +33% (one of three raising, none cutting), unchanged on 4 and 5-Nov. EPS and EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth fell from +67% at the 30-Sep month-end to +33% at 31-Oct as a September raise left the counting window; revenue and PT have been +33% since 30-Sep. There has been no Sell in 24 months. With no broker cutting there is no stated logic to report; an AlphaSense search filed 3-Nov found no research from the eight houses that cover Arteche.

In your 28 to 31-Oct note you wrote: "SS EPS breadth turned negative in Jun-Aug 26. Unclear since then. Data issue". The data file shows a real cut, not a data issue: EPS Net Upgrade Breadth was (50%) at the June and July 2026 month-ends (one of two analysts cutting, as FY2027 consensus EPS fell 2.7%), then +33%, +67% and +33% from August to October. The only oddity is two analysts counted in June and July against three otherwise.

Arteche's average PT (€39.11) is 41.2% above the SP (€27.7), the widest gap at any reading since August 2024.

  • The PT has followed the SP: it rose 58% from the March 2026 month-end to 29-Sep, in steps after SP rises (€24.8 to €34.2 over the May and June 2026 month-ends, after the May spike), and has not moved since 29-Sep while the SP fell 26.5%.
  • Precedent: the earlier gaps above 20% (February and March 2025; August 2026) closed through SP rises, but opened on rising estimates; this one opened on flat estimates.

Analysts answered a third-quarter profit beat with EBITDA cuts for every year: they read the beat as timing, not as a better year. (Alfen's years to December; FactSet means; EUR; change from the 31-Oct reading to 5-Nov; the FY2027 cuts are dated 4-Nov in the daily file.)

  • EBITDA: FY2026 (2.2%) to €21.65m, FY2027 (2.5%) to €27.97m, FY2028 (1.8%) to €33.77m. Over 3 months FY2026 is down 14.4%, FY2027 9.0% and FY2028 6.4%; over 12 months FY2027 is down 28.1%.
  • Revenue: up 0.6% for each year, to €458.1m (FY2026), €495.5m (FY2027) and €537.2m (FY2028).
  • EPS: the FY2026 loss narrowed 6.2% to €(0.140); FY2027 fell 1.3% to €0.350; FY2028 fell 7.8% to €0.460. Six analysts now carry an FY2027 EPS estimate, from seven.

The arithmetic of the cut (my calculation from the third-quarter figures in Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026 and the earnings review). First-half adjusted EBITDA was €16.4m and the third quarter's €4.0m, so nine months delivered €20.4m. The €21.65m FY2026 consensus therefore implies a fourth quarter of about €1.25m. Before the results, consensus of €22.15m with a third-quarter expectation of €2.3m implied a fourth quarter of about €3.45m. Analysts took about €2.2m out of the fourth quarter, more than the €1.7m third-quarter beat. Implied fourth-quarter revenue is €97.5m, against €99.1m in the third quarter.

The later years are cut, so analysts are lowering the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EBITDA margins fell to 4.7% (FY2026), 5.6% (FY2027) and 6.3% (FY2028), from 4.9%, 5.8% and 6.4% on 31-Oct. Implied EBITDA growth is 29.2% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 20.7% to FY2028, on revenue growth of 8.2% and 8.4%. The pattern since the half-year results of 18-Aug: more revenue at a lower margin.

The links: the cuts followed the price fall, and the memo's first downgrade step has come, smaller than the memo's. Your note of 3-Nov, "Almost always, price moves first", fits: Alfen's SP fell 18.8% from 30-Sep to 26-Oct with FY2027 EPS and revenue consensus unchanged and FY2027 EBITDA down only 0.3%, and the cuts came on 4-Nov. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) forecast "three downgrade steps ending in August 2027", starting with an FY2026 EBITDA cut (its FY2026 forecast is €18.3m, against €21.65m consensus) and an FY2027 EPS reset, which it calls "about 85% too high" on depreciation. Measured against the memo: FY2026 EBITDA is now 18% above the memo's forecast, and FY2027 EPS has fallen only 1.3%. Against the memo: the third quarter's €4.0m alone exceeds the memo's whole second-half forecast of about €1.9m.

A point to confirm: the lower end of Alfen's FY2026 revenue guidance. Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026 (Jefferies) gives the reiterated FY2026 revenue guidance as €425m to €475m; the earnings review gives it before the update as €435m to €475m. If both are right, the bottom of the range fell by €10m.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus has not moved since the 31-Oct month-end, while the SP fell 6.8% from the 30-Oct close; the estimates still rise at every horizon over 3 and 6 months, at a pace slowed to low single digits a month.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521: +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1% (first estimate 30-Apr).
  • Volex's FY2027 revenue, $1,411m: +1.8%, +6.8%, +12.2%; FY2027 EBITDA, $192.6m: +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%. FY2028 and FY2029 revenue and EBITDA rise at about the same pace.
  • Implied growth: FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth is 11.1% (11.7% at the 31-Jul month-end) and FY2028 to FY2029 10.3% (11.6%). The later years rise a little more slowly than FY2027 (EPS +15.0%, +14.4% and +13.1% over 3 months for FY2027, FY2028 and FY2029), so the implied growth has narrowed: analysts are raising the level faster than the trajectory, a mild pulling forward of earnings.
  • Implied margin: Volex's FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.7% (13.2% at 31-Jul) and FY2028's 13.8%; the upgrades are margin first.
  • A data point: the data file warns its daily estimate series are not joined to the month-end series, so the flat week may be the feed.

Volex's SP led its estimates up to 6-Oct; since then the SP has fallen 25.0% while FY2027 EPS rose about 3%. Hypothesis: the market is discounting the FY2028 data-centre risk consensus does not show: the largest customer (18.7% of FY2026 revenue, about the size of the data-centre line) on one-to-two-year contracts as a secondary supplier into the 2026 to 2028 design re-award window (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov). Against it: the 19-Oct update guided FY2027 profit "towards the upper end" and every analyst raised. Tests at the 12-Nov results (the memo's): H1 underlying operating profit at or above $74m; working capital falling from 28.4% of revenue; the data-centre share of Complex Industrial Technology restated.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Volex passes it: FY2027 EPS +15.0% over 3 months and +20.0% over 6 months, 7 of 7 analysts raising. The memo expects the 3-month figure to fall to low single digits by late January on window arithmetic alone.

The price is a little stronger than flat (+3.1% on the week, +5.0% on the month), but there is no higher high: 5-Nov's intraday 269.2 closed back at 265.0, below 270.6.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Sdiptech against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes, +3.5% over 21 sessions
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes, 244.80 against 229.82
  • The 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA: passes, 254.60

  • Near its 52W high: passes, 2.1% below 270.6

  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: partly; 26-Oct's (4.2%), after a sales beat, is the largest one-day fall since 24-Jul; the largest five-session fall, (6.1%) to 14-Sep, came earlier
  • Wider swings: partly; 23 and 26-Oct were wide (7.8% and 3.8% ranges); the last five sessions average 2.7% a day, narrower than the 4.0% of the five sessions before (23 to 29-Oct) but wider than the 2.5% of the 50 sessions to 22-Oct

Verdict: a healthy retest, with three of your Stage 3 (topping) signs (22-Sep) partly present. The reading that settles it: a close above 270.6 on 1.5x the 50D average volume; a close below 252.4 and the 50D MA on heavy volume would make 26-Oct the start of a top.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Volume ratios are the volume script's, in shares, without the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's own ratios differ by 0.02x at most.

Sdiptech's recovery to within 2.1% of its high has come on drying volume, and the last three sessions closed in the lower half of their ranges as the intraday highs rose. No session since 27-Oct has traded above 1.2x the 50D average; the seven sessions to 5-Nov averaged about 56,000 shares, 0.69x the 50D average of about 81,500 shares (about SEK 20.6m a day). The intraday highs rose from 265.8 (3-Nov) to 267.4 and 269.2 (5-Nov), 0.5% short of the 270.6 closing high, while the closes sat 42%, 48% and 32% up the day's range. Hypothesis: supply waits near 270, where the heavy-volume days of 14 and 15-Oct traded. Against: a quiet approach to a high is also how a base tightens before a breakout. Test: the volume on the first close above 270.6.

The heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions: the selling came with the industry, the buying was Sdiptech's own, with one exception.

  • 7-Oct: (1.4%) on 305,630 shares (SEK 77.1m), 4.33x, closing 20% up its range; its industry fell 2.3%, cohort peers 2.5%, and Alimak (4.7x), Bravida and Addtech (2.3x each) also traded heavily: a Swedish industrial sell-off
  • 14-Oct: +5.9% to the 270.6 high on 140,174 shares (SEK 37.9m), 1.87x, closing 97% up its range after a 1.3% gap up; industry +0.2%; cause not in the sources read
  • 15-Oct: (1.9%) on 150,360 shares (SEK 39.9m), 1.95x, closing mid-range, industry (1.5%): the breakout partly reversed on heavier volume
  • 23-Oct, the third-quarter report: +4.6% on 240,370 shares (SEK 63.5m), 3.04x; it traded down to 246.2, below the 50D and 100D MAs, and closed 90% up its range
  • 26-Oct, the exception: (4.2%) on 120,761 shares (SEK 30.6m), 1.49x (1.50x on the dashboard), closing at its low while its industry rose 1.4% and all three cohort peers rose
  • 27-Oct: +2.4% on 157,035 shares (SEK 40.7m), 1.88x, closing at its high after a 0.9% gap down, industry +1.2%

Distribution has thinned, by old days leaving the count rather than by new buying. The PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts distribution days (your definition: a down day on 1.25x or more the 50D average volume) over the last 25 sessions: 3 in the 25 sessions to 5-Nov (7, 15 and 26-Oct), against 5 in the 25 sessions to 22-Oct and in the 25 sessions to 29-Oct. The two that left the count, 25-Sep (2.1x) and 1-Oct (5.5x), dropped out of the 25-session window on 30-Oct and 5-Nov; no distribution day has come since 26-Oct. The volume script flags 7, 15 and 26-Oct as distribution days and 12, 14, 23 and 27-Oct as accumulation days; none of either since 27-Oct.

Up-day volume leads over 20 sessions, not over the whole advance. The 20-session up/down volume ratio is 1.41, from 0.86 on 27-Oct; the dashboard's 5-session ratio reads 0.77, but the chart file's last five sessions give 1.83 (164,685 shares on up days against 90,109 on down days), so the 0.77 is a data point to check, not evidence of selling. The advance from the 24-Jul low (+14.2% in 74 sessions) has an up/down volume ratio of 0.97, against 1.27 for the February to April advance and 1.33 for May to June: Sdiptech's least-sponsored advance of 2026.

At the MA tests the rising 50D MA held three times, once on heavy selling. 22-Oct closed on it (252.4 against 252.35) on 0.44x; 26-Oct closed 0.05% above it on 1.49x, the nearest to failing; on 2-Nov the SP traded to 252.6, below it, and closed 3.3% above on 0.48x.

Precedent: October's low ended like February's on the first day and like May's since. The 9-Feb low ended with a 13.2% rise on 6.1x, then a 45.6% advance; the 5-May and 24-Jul lows ended on light volume and led to 18.2% and 16.6% advances. October's ended with the 3.04x reversal of 23-Oct and the 1.88x accumulation day of 27-Oct, then a light drift up. The October 2025 path (a report-day spike, then (15.5%) in 10 sessions) has not repeated: nine sessions later Sdiptech is 4.7% above its 26-Oct close.

Relative price confirmed the 14-Oct high: it made its 52W high that day, is 4.8% below it on 5-Nov, and 8.7% above its relative 200D MA, which rose 2.70% over the month. No cohort peer traded heavily with Sdiptech on 14, 23 or 27-Oct, except Storskogen on its own report day (1.7x on 23-Oct).

Confirms the advance resumes: a close above 270.6 on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (about 122,000 shares, SEK 33m). Confirms a top: a close below the 50D MA (254.6) and the 22-Oct low (252.4) on 1.5x or more.

3. RS

Sdiptech's Stock RS and its Industry RS have moved apart for four weeks, to the widest gap in the data file: Stock RS 88, its 13-month high, against Industry RS 26, its 13-month low.

  • Stock RS: 88 on 5-Nov, from 84 at the 30-Oct month-end, 85 a month ago (2-Oct), 70 three months ago (31-Jul) and 12 a year ago (31-Oct-2025); 70 or more at every reading since 25-Sep
  • Sector RS (Diversified industrial groups): 72, from 80 on 4-Nov, down from its 13-month high of 91 (6-Oct); 22 points above the line of 50, but it moved 8 points in one session
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 26 on 4 and 5-Nov, from 46 on 3-Nov and at the month-end, 79 on 9-Oct and 59 three months ago; the previous 13-month low was 40 (March 2026). It fell three places among 16 industries on 4-Nov alone, when industrials fell 0.3% and the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8%

The order of the turn: the industry first (after 9-Oct), the sector second (after 6 and 16-Oct), Sdiptech not at all. On the market chart industrials fell 1.1% over the month while Consumer staples rose 10.7% and Healthcare 9.3%; Sdiptech rose 5.0%. In its cohort only Sdiptech's Stock RS is higher than a month ago (Indus Holding 90 from 97, Storskogen 67 from 75).

Friday 6-Nov. Industry RS is a rank among 16 industries, in steps of about 6.7; 26 is 12th place, and 11th place (about 33) clears the line of 30. The APM's replica of the ranking (a weighted return over 3, 6, 9 and 12 months) has been within one place of every reading since 2-Oct and exact on 4 and 5-Nov. In the replica, industrials stay 12th on a flat Friday; to reach 11th they must beat Utilities, now 11th, by about 0.6 of a percentage point on the day (a replica score gap of 0.62 points, 3.54 against 4.16). Industrials beat Utilities by that much on about one session in four over the past year. Probable: a reading below 30 on Friday 6-Nov, but the margin is narrower than one percentage point.

The precedent runs against the inference that a weak industry drags Sdiptech: at the March 2026 low in Industry RS (40), Sdiptech's Stock RS rose from 38 to 56 inside its 45.6% advance. The rule reads the industry, not the stock.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts are cutting Sdiptech's FY2026 EPS and raising FY2027 and FY2028 faster than before: a raised trajectory beyond a weaker current year.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS consensus (8.0%) in a month to SEK 13.16 (from 14.30 at 30-Sep; (1.1%) on 5-Nov alone), which reverses an 8.1% rise in September (from 13.23 at 31-Aug), so it is (0.5%) over 3 months and (15.5%) over 12 months; revenue +2.9% to SEK 5,210m and EBITDA +3.1% to SEK 1,214m: EBITDA consensus rose, so the EPS cut comes from items below EBITDA
  • FY2027: EPS +2.1% in a month to SEK 17.79 (+7.9% over 3 months); revenue +5.2% to SEK 5,647m (+6.8%); EBITDA +2.8% to SEK 1,416m (+4.0%)
  • FY2028: EPS +3.7% in a month to SEK 19.97 (+9.2% over 3 months); revenue +5.9% to SEK 6,047m; EBITDA +3.4% to SEK 1,523m

The later year rises faster, so analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 rose from 10.5% (30-Sep) to 12.3% (5-Nov); implied revenue growth from FY2026 to FY2027 from 6.1% to 8.4%.

The margin keeps slipping. Sdiptech's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) is 25.1%, from 25.7% at 30-Sep and 25.9% at 30-Apr; FY2028's is 25.2% from 25.8%. The third-quarter report (23-Oct) explains it: organic sales growth of 13% against DNB Carnegie's 6%, but an adjusted EBITA margin of 20.0% against a 20.9% consensus, on Supply Chain & Transportation (18.5% from 21.0%, raw-material costs at Mecno), which management said would weigh "through the fourth quarter of 2026" (earnings review).

The FY2026 EPS cut has named parts worth about 3%, not 8%. The SEK 13m bond-redemption charge cut 2026 adjusted EPS by about 2%, and DNB Carnegie trimmed its 2026 adjusted EPS by 1% on higher segment costs (earnings review). Hypothesis: the rest is definitional: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (31-Oct) calls FactSet's EPS consensus "a median of two brokers on two different EPS definitions", so one broker's update moves it several percent. For it: FY2026 EPS consensus rose 8.1% in one step on 30-Sep and has given it back since, leaving it (0.5%) over 3 months. Test: FY2027 EPS rising while FY2026 settles.

The SP followed the estimates this month, where in September it led them (+12.4% from 15-Sep to 14-Oct against +1.2% for FY2027 revenue consensus from 30-Sep to 14-Oct (+2.7% from 31-Aug)). The memo's tracking line (31-Oct): organic adjusted EBITA growth below 3% for two quarters "changes the case", and fourth-quarter cash conversion below 90% is "the warning"; the third quarter gave 6% and 62% (a seasonal low, 94% a year earlier).

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Net Upgrade Breadth is broad on revenue and EBITDA and narrow on EPS and PT, matching item 4 (Consensus by named FY): upgrades that stop short of earnings and value.

  • Revenue (FY2027): +83% on 5-Nov (5 raising, none cutting, of 6), from +67% at the September and October month-ends
  • EBITDA (FY2027): +83% (5 of 6 raising), from +67% in September
  • EPS (FY2027): +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +80% in September and +100% in April and May
  • PT: +43% (3 raising, none cutting, of 7), from +100% in May and June
  • Buys: 5 of 6; the first non-Buy in 24 months appeared at the October month-end

Where analysts are cutting, and why. One analyst cut FY2027 EPS; the sources read do not name which, nor the analyst behind the non-Buy. DNB Carnegie (Carl Korsheden) trimmed 2026 adjusted EPS 1% on higher segment costs, kept 2027 at SEK 19.5 and cut his PT to SEK 340 from 342, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples and compressed earnings yield spreads in the capital goods sector" (earnings review, 23-Oct). Hypothesis: Sdiptech fits the pattern the Sector Digest (30-Oct) sees across Swedish serial acquirers, PTs trimmed on peer multiples while estimates rise. Against it, RedEye raised its base case to SEK 315 (29-Oct, Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026).

The average PT fell 1.5% when a seventh analyst arrived, not on a cut. It moved from 312.7 (26 to 28-Oct, 6 analysts) to 308.1 (2-Nov, 7 analysts) with no PT cut counted. If the other six were unchanged, the seventh analyst's PT is about SEK 281 (7 times 308.1 less 6 times 312.7), 6% above the close: the most cautious view on Sdiptech, and possibly the source of the non-Buy.

Ratings carry no signal; the PT does the work. Every analyst in the data file (ratings from October 2024) rated Sdiptech a Buy through its 35% fall from 258.2 (11-Nov-2024) to 167.0 (9-Feb-2026) and its rise since. The average PT followed the SP, down 35% from 398.8 (August 2024) to 258.4 (January 2026) and up 19% since.

Hypothesis: the average PT has capped Sdiptech's SP when the PT stood about 12% to 16% above it, and the SP is now at the edge of that zone. Three times in 2026 Sdiptech came that close (25-May, PT 12.1% above the close; the August month-end, 15.8%; 14-Oct, 12.7%), fell 6.6% to 7.8%, and analysts then raised the PT. On 5-Nov the PT is 16.3% above the close and was 14.5% above the 269.2 intraday high. Test: a close above 270.6 with no PT rise breaks the pattern; a third stall near 270 with the PT flat fits it.

Precedent: Sdiptech's SP has turned with breadth (January to April 2026: EPS breadth from (100%) to +100%, PT from (40%) to +83%, SP +45.6%). Test: EPS and PT Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Nov month-end.

9. Peers

Sdiptech leads its cohort (Diversified serial acquirers in a reset) on SP, RS and estimates; the cohort's other leader, Indus Holding, is the one turning down. Your four peer checks:

  • SP technicals: from 26-Oct to 5-Nov Indus Holding fell 4.8% and closed 5.3% below its 50D MA (Stock RS 90 from 97 a month ago) while Sdiptech rose 4.7%; Storskogen rose 3.4% (Stock RS 67 from 75); Volati (Stock RS 0) remains the control case
  • Sell-side estimates: 3-month EPS revisions are Sdiptech +8%, Indus Holding +5%, Storskogen (1%), Volati (4%); Sdiptech is the only cohort member with estimates and Stock RS both rising
  • Newsflow: Lifco fell 12.0% on 23-Oct on 6.5 times its volume, organic growth 1.6% (Sector Digest, 30-Oct); DNB Carnegie cut Lifco's 2027 and 2028 EPS 5% to 6% and its PT to SEK 338 on customer losses while calling Sdiptech's quarter clean (earnings review). Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026 had no item on Sdiptech's peers, and nothing read covers 5-Nov
  • Guidance and company communications: no peer guidance change is in the sources read; Indus Holding reports on 12-Nov, the cohort's next dated test

The Swedish serial acquirers bounced together in the week to 5-Nov while their industry fell. From 29-Oct Sdiptech rose 3.1%, Storskogen 3.7% and Lifco 3.5%, while industrials fell 1.5% and the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 0.9%; on 4-Nov Lifco rose 3.5%, Storskogen 2.4% and Sdiptech 1.0% with industrials (0.3%). Hypothesis: a bid for Swedish compounders after Lifco's collapse, not Sdiptech news. Against: Lifco's Stock RS is 18 (46 a month ago), and one week is thin. Test: whether they diverge on Indus Holding's 12-Nov report.

Sdiptech's sector is splitting by size. Over the month the large diversified groups lost Stock RS (thyssenkrupp 92 to 59, Smiths Group 74 to 39, Hill & Smith 90 to 60, TKH 92 to 69, Aalberts 91 to 71) while the Nordic small and mid-caps gained (Schouw & Co 88 to 92, Röko 52 to 67, Sdiptech up to 88). That is why Sector RS (72) holds up better than Industry RS (26).

No stock outside the cohort tracks Sdiptech closely: the closest, Munters (average correlation 0.26, weak), fell 6.0% on 4-Nov and 4.2% on 5-Nov (cause not in the sources read) while Sdiptech rose.

What the peers imply: Sdiptech's SP and estimates are carried by its own report, and the peers show no cohort-wide selling that would reach its Stock RS or the 30%-fall test on its tranches soon. They cannot help Friday's Industry RS, which the industry's large members set. Test: Indus Holding on 12-Nov; a miss that Sdiptech follows down on heavy volume would show the cohort's leaders sold together.

What the rules say for Sdiptech now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • The industry, not Sdiptech, is about to decide the holding. Industry RS for Industrials and capital goods read 26 on 4 and 5-Nov; a Friday 6-Nov reading below 30 is a whole-holding sell signal. Probable: industrials stay 12th of 16 unless they beat Utilities, 11th, by about 0.6 of a percentage point on the day, which they have done on about one session in four over the past year, as item 3 (RS) shows. It is not a sell signal until Friday's reading
  • Every Sdiptech-specific reading is clear, most improving. Stock RS 88 is its 13-month high; the rising 50D MA held through the post-report swings; all six trend-health tests pass; FY2027 and FY2028 EPS consensus rose 2.1% and 3.7% in a month
  • The negatives are in the detail. The SP is approaching its 270.6 high on 0.69x volume and closing in the lower half of its ranges; FY2026 EPS fell 8.0% in a month (a reversal of September's 8.1% rise; (0.5%) over 3 months) and the FY2027 margin keeps slipping; EPS and PT Net Upgrade Breadth have halved since the spring; the PT is 16.3% above the close (14.5% above the 5-Nov intraday high), near the 12% to 16% gap at which Sdiptech stalled three times in 2026

Each tranche today: hold all five (tranche 1, 3.21%; tranche 2, 1.14%; tranche 3, 1.09%; tranche 4, 1.04%; tranche 5, 1.01%). A Middle Innings Core Investment is sold only by the six Sell Criteria, and none gives a sell signal at its last reading.

The nearest sell tests:

  • Industry RS below 30, Friday 6-Nov: 53 a week ago (29-Oct), 79 four weeks ago (9-Oct), 53 thirteen weeks ago (6-Aug), 26 now. No Sdiptech move is needed. If below 30: the rules sell the whole holding, all five tranches (7.49%), at the Tuesday 10-Nov session; Sdiptech is not under the one-off in/out link, so no replacement comes first. Volex gives the same sell signal on the same reading. If 30 or more: the test repeats on Friday 13-Nov
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, 30-Nov month-end: 4.8% below now, 6.8% a week ago, 3.0% four weeks ago, 10.1% thirteen weeks ago; at a flat market only a close near 208.7, 21.2% lower, gives a sell signal
  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70, 30-Nov: Sector RS must fall 23 points and Stock RS 19
  • How they interact: industrial weakness moves Industry RS and Sector RS together, but Sdiptech's Stock RS has ignored both for four weeks; the month-end tests also need a Sdiptech fall of over 20%, so Industry RS comes first by weeks

The record: Industry RS has not been this low in the data file's 13 months, so the rule has no precedent on this holding. At the previous low of 40 (March 2026, described in item 3 (RS)), the rule would not have given a sell signal.

For your ruling, the size: the register records tranche 5 at 1.00% at entry and the holding at 7.49%, above the 6.68% limit; on 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I added 60bps at ~266 SEK at market to take position to 6.0% of AUM". The UBS weight of Sdiptech at 30-Oct was 5.21% of AUM, against 6.24% in the register. If the holding is 6.0% of AUM, as your note says, it sits 0.68% below its 6.68% limit.

The next reading day and the add: Friday 6-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA (rising 2.70% over the month; no risk). The rules block an add today, because the register puts Sdiptech above its limit. Where Sdiptech stands for an add: basing, the first stage (pulling back or basing), in a 16-session range from 252.4 to 270.6 below its 14-Oct high, after a held retest of its rising 50D MA. The Master Dashboard reads none of the three (pulling back 2 of 4, basing 2 of 4, healthy retest 3 of 6 on the 50D MA, VCP 0 of 4); recent ranges have narrowed since the report week (2.7% a day over the last five sessions against 4.0% over the five before) but are no tighter than before the report (2.5%). Richard decides.

Exosens is Theon's closest match on own daily moves (average rank correlation 0.57, strong), and the link is in the business: Theon holds 4.98M Exosens shares (9.8%) and is about 40% of Exosens' revenue (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo). Exosens' 10.2% fall to €52.15 cut the stake by about €29m, 1.2% of Theon's €2.41bn market value at 29-Oct: about one of Theon's 10.5 points.

Hypotheses for Theon's extra fall, ranked: 1. A small, levered member of a de-risking industry, sold harder than the group. For it: Theon fell 1.96 times Defence's week but rose only 1.15 times it in October (item 1 (SP and MAs)), and Exosens and Kongsberg Gruppen fell with it on heavy volume on 5-Nov. Against it: the 28-Oct fall ((4.6%), cohort +1.3%) was Theon's own. 2. Read-across from German call-off delays to Theon's leverage test. For it: the memo prices "a one-quarter slip in OCCAR or German call-offs" at EPS (6%), +0.2 times leverage and a missed 2.5 times target. Against it: Hensoldt, more exposed to German budgets, rose on 5-Nov, and no source links Rheinmetall's Boxer delay to Theon's programmes.

What the peers imply: with Industry RS at 0, Theon's direction rests on the group; a day on which Theon rises while Exosens and Kongsberg Gruppen fall would be the first sign of decoupling. The next peer tests: the Bundestag budget committee (12-Nov) and vote (27-Nov) (Industry Digest).

What the rules say for Avanza now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: the rules still sell Avanza. Two whole-holding Sell Criteria gave sell signals at the 30-Oct month-end, and under your one-off in/out link Avanza is sold when its replacement is bought, in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale.

  • On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I understand the SL rules (largely linked to Stock RS at 37) say it should be sold". Confirmed, with one correction: the sale comes from two Sell Criteria, not an SL (MA SLs do not apply to a Core Investment). At 30-Oct Sector RS was 18 with Stock RS 33, and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (16%) and SS Sales (6%) with Stock RS 33.
  • Your 4-Nov purchase: you added 1.0% of capital (tranche 1), taking Avanza to 4.11% now. The rules blocked an add that day because a Sell Criterion had given a sell signal; the purchase is your decision.
  • Each tranche today: tranches 1 (1.00%), 2 (0.47% now, 0.50% at entry) and 3 (2.64% now, 2.57% at entry) are held until a replacement is bought, then sold with the holding. No sale is due at Tue 10-Nov: the order state's "SELL TRANCHE(S) 1 on Tuesday without a replacement" comes from tranche 1's broken purchase date (0002-11-04), a data fault.
  • The relative 200D MA (read each Friday): +0.145% over the month on 5-Nov, from +0.240% a week earlier and (0.44%) thirteen weeks earlier.   • Sector RS with Stock RS (read 30-Nov): to stop giving a sell signal at 30-Nov it needs Sector RS at 50 or Stock RS at 70. Plausible: Sector RS rose 35 points in three weeks from 27-Feb-2026.
  • SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero with Stock RS below 50 (read 30-Nov): Stock RS rose 7 points since the 30-Oct month-end (33 to 40), but is down 15 over the month (from 55) and 24 over three months (from 64), on the dashboard's one- and three-month readings. Plausible: Stock RS rose 30 points in four weeks from 27-Feb-2026.

Bally's Intralot

Middle Innings Core Investment · 1.88% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 1.88%, bought 26-Jun-2026) · readings as at Thu 5-Nov-2026

Two sessions since the previous note (3-Nov close), and only one of them is in the data: the chart files of Bally's Intralot and of every Athens-listed stock end on 4-Nov, and the data file carries the 4-Nov close (€1.119) into its 5-Nov row, which is a data fault. Closes, legs and MAs use the data file's unrounded closes on Athens trading days (the chart file rounds Bally's Intralot to €0.01, about 0.9%).

1. SP and MAs

Bally's Intralot fell on 4-Nov while the market, its industry and Athens rose: a small fall, but the stock's own, and it took relative price below its relative 200D MA. The SP closed at €1.119 on 4-Nov, (0.7%) on the session, while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% and Consumer discretionary 2.5%. At a beta of 0.66 the market would have lifted Bally's Intralot about 1.2%, so about (1.9) percentage points of the day's fall was the stock's own (the (0.7%) fall less the 1.2% the market would have added), about half its usual weekly move of 3.6%. The median of the 24 Athens-listed stocks in the chart files rose 0.5% that day; Allwyn, the other Athens-listed gambling stock, fell 1.0%.

  • The trend: +0.2% over five sessions (after taking out the market's part, the stock's own move was (1.2) percentage points, 0.3 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%), (2.6%) over 3 months and +2.8% over 12 months, between €0.831 (30-Mar) and €1.197 (23-Jun).
  • The legs since the August high: €1.176 (14-Aug) to €1.059 (18-Sep), (9.9%); to €1.140 (1-Oct), +7.6%; to €1.080 (8-Oct), (5.3%); to €1.130 (21-Oct), +4.6%; to €1.119 (4-Nov), (1.0%). Every close since 23-Oct sits between €1.117 and €1.127.
  • On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Bally's SP flat L1W. Not doing much". Still true of the SP, which has moved 0.2% in five sessions; it is no longer true of relative price, which fell 2.2% on 4-Nov, its largest one-day relative fall since the 18-Sep low.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run; the newest read is Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026 (about 28-Oct to 4-Nov). Its one Bally's Intralot item, in the previous note, is the 2-Nov press release that Deutsche Bank bought 955,000 shares on 26 and 30-Oct at an average €1.11 under the company's total return equity swap, shares that return to Bally's Intralot as own shares on settlement; it records no sell-side rating change.

On the same day Bally's Corporation, the 58% parent, fell 6.4% and Evoke, the company Bally's Intralot is buying for shares, fell 2.5% (peers' chart files); the causes of both are not in the sources read. The MAs: the long MAs rise, the medium MAs fall, and the SP slipped back below the 100D MA. On 4-Nov: 100D €1.123 (falling 0.7% over 21 sessions), 20D €1.113 (rising 1.9%), 150D €1.113 (rising 3.0%), 50D €1.106 (falling 0.9%), absolute 200D €1.071 (rising 0.7%). Partly: the 150D MA is 3.9% above the absolute 200D MA and both rise, but the 50D MA sits 0.6% below the 150D MA and 1.5% below the 100D MA and falls, so the stack is not in Stage 2 order.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Bally's Intralot against your trend-health tests (a sideways base, not a pullback):

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+0.7% over 21 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€1.113 against €1.071).

Each high since 14-Aug has been lower, which is not one of your red flags but caps the base.

What settles it: a close above €1.140 (the 1-Oct high) on 1.5 times the 50D average volume or more; or a close below €1.11, the swap's price, and then €1.080 (the 8-Oct low) on heavy volume.

The 50D average holds two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug; 18-Sep, an index day), which lower each ratio by about 10%; the volume script's figure without them is in brackets. Close-in-range readings rest on the chart file's rounded prices and are coarse.

The base is still being held at €1.11 on falling volume, and 4-Nov added a distribution day on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's count.

On your own definition (a down day at 1.25 times the 50D average or more) it is not one, and 13-Oct stays the only distribution day in 25 sessions.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Generally, over L6M, down days have much higher volume than up days - a fragility indicator IMHO". Confirmed in total, less so per day. Over 126 sessions there were 67 down days against 53 up days, and down days averaged 8% more volume than up days (3.05M against 2.82M shares), so total down-day volume was 1.37 times total up-day volume. Over 63 sessions the figures are 32 against 27 and 7%. The fragility comes mostly from the number of down days.

Precedent: the two declines since June were one-sided (up/down volume 0.19 each, rounded count); three of the four declines that ended in the past year turned with an up day at 1.2 to 1.9 times the 50D average within three sessions of the low (27 and 28-Nov-2025; 5-Mar-2026; 27-Jul-2026); the March 2026 low turned on light volume (+8.6% on 0.8x on 31-Mar). Bally's Intralot's relative price fell 2.2% on 4-Nov to 0.6080, 1.4% below its relative 200D MA (0.6168), its fifth crossing of that MA since 29-Oct; the 5-Nov row (0.6102, 1.1% below) moves only because the market fell 0.4% on 5-Nov while Bally's Intralot's close in the data is the stale 4-Nov one. What the peers imply: the sector's turn is under way without Bally's Intralot, whose daily moves correlate only weakly with any stock (CrediaBank, 0.24, the strongest): a stock with its own overhangs (leverage, the parent, the Evoke share issue), not one waiting for its industry.

  • Finding 1, the 4-Nov fall was Bally's Intralot's own: (0.7%) while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% and Consumer discretionary 2.5%; relative price fell 2.2% to below its relative 200D MA, and Stock RS fell from 47 to 40 (items 1 to 3).

1. SP and MAs

Hexatronic's SP fell 19.5% in the five sessions to 5-Nov (47.28 to 38.06), its largest five-session fall since the advance began at the 18-Nov-2025 low; the market explains none of it. Beta (1.15) on a rising market predicts a gain of 1.0 point; the stock-specific part of the fall is therefore (20.5) points, 2.3 times Hexatronic's usual weekly move (8.8%). Hexatronic is up 85.6% over 12 months and down 2.4% over 3 months (over the same 3 months Technology fell 2.0% and the Stoxx Europe 600 0.8%); it closes 21.7% below its 52W closing high (48.58, 3-Jun) and below its pre-report close of 39.00 (21-Oct): the whole gain from the third-quarter report is gone.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run; the report of 4-Nov-2026 has nothing on Hexatronic beyond a 30-Oct share registration ("No new broker research reports or rating revisions"). 4. Consensus by named FY

Hexatronic's SP fell 15.2% on 4 and 5-Nov with no change in any estimate: the SP is moving ahead of the estimates.

  • Hexatronic's FY2026 to FY2028 EPS, revenue and EBITDA and its average PT have not moved since 2-Nov (FY2027 EPS SEK 2.946, revenue SEK 9,561m, EBITDA SEK 1,342m)
  • On 3-Nov you wrote in your market note: "Almost always, price moves first". The fall is either the market pricing a cut not yet visible, or a de-rating on unchanged earnings; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (25-Oct) records that in 2023 "the multiple fell one to two quarters before earnings did", and judges "a 20-35% drawdown on intact fundamentals" plausible with "one visible broker and no anchor owner"

Analysts lifted the near year faster than the far year after the report: earnings pulled forward, not a higher trajectory.

  • One month (30-Sep to 5-Nov): EPS FY2026 +7.2%, FY2027 +10.3% (SEK 2.672 to 2.946), FY2028 +7.0% (3.072 to 3.286); revenue +3.3%, +3.8%, +3.2%; EBITDA +5.1%, +4.5%, +3.5%
  • Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 narrowed from 15.0% to 11.5%, because FY2027 EPS rose faster than FY2028 EPS; from February to August the two years had risen at the same pace
  • Implied EBITDA margins: FY2026 13.4%, FY2027 14.0% (13.9% a month ago), FY2028 14.3%. FY2027 EPS rose 10.3% against 4.5% for FY2027 EBITDA, more than twice as fast: the extra gain comes from below the EBITDA line, not from margin

The revision burst the memo expected has happened and is larger, which leaves consensus above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's own view. The memo expected "a +5-9% revision burst", "the Q3 print being recorded, not new information"; FY2027 EPS rose 10.3%. Consensus FY2027 EPS now sits 6.0% above the memo's SEK 2.78 and 9% above its probability-weighted SEK 2.7, and the memo puts the chance of a cut at "about 40%" at each report from mid-2027. The upgrade momentum that carried the SP since February has no scheduled support before the 4-Feb-2027 report.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "FX is likely to be a big headwind, similar to Xvivo - major fundamental risk; read the memo". The memo's figure: a 10% stronger krona removes about 9% of reported revenue but only about 3% of EPS, "because production is local". With the Riksbank's rise of 4-Nov, the krona is a small earnings risk for Hexatronic and a larger sentiment risk (item 1 (SP and MAs), SP and MAs).

For the holding: the economic peers' guidance is strong and their SPs are falling, the pattern the Sector Digest (13-Oct) called "AI monetization anxiety", not weaker optical numbers

  • Nokia: Stock RS 41 from 93 a month ago, 14.9% below its 50D MA and 16.7% below its absolute 200D MA (to 4-Nov); its 52W high came on 3-Jun, the day of Hexatronic's. Nokia's 3M EPS revision is +4% and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth 52%: like Hexatronic, its estimates rose while its SP broke
  • Ericsson moves the other way (Stock RS 49 from 34, 4.1% above its 50D MA), though it fell 4.2% on 5-Nov; Adtran Networks (Stock RS 26) moves with neither
  • The precedent that matches: Nokia's Stock RS has fallen 52 points in a month, in a break that began with a 9.8% fall on its 22-Oct report while its estimates rose. Hexatronic's 3M EPS revision (+11%) is the strongest of the cohort, which is what separated it from Nokia in October

Hexatronic's daily moves still track Mycronic (correlation 0.64), which fell 3.5% on each day and 11.8% over the week; on 4-Nov domestic Sweden rose (Investor +1.4%, Indutrade +2.0%), but Atlas Copco fell only 1.5% and Sdiptech rose 1.0%, so the Swedish-rates hypothesis (item 1 (SP and MAs), SP and MAs) is the weaker one.

On 5-Nov the selling widened to the sector (Kontron (7.4%) on 4.4x, secunet Security Networks (6.1%), Kitron (4.4%)); Hexatronic's Stock RS (82) is now level with secunet's (81), after standing alone at the top in October.

  • The three years rose at about the same pace, again lifting the level, not the trajectory. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.6% for EPS, 4.9% for EBITDA and 4.7% for revenue (FY2027 to FY2028: EPS 9.2%, EBITDA 5.9%, revenue 5.2%); the implied EBITDA margin is 11.5% for FY2026 and FY2027 and 11.6% for FY2028
  • The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's test: FY2026 EBITDA rose 1.1% on revenue up 0.2%, so the revising analysts added profit, not sales. Hypothesis: IMCD's third-quarter conversion margin did not miss the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's 42% line. For it: the first day's revisions sit close to the memo's base case for 5-Nov ("EPS +1-2%; FY2027 held or nudged up by three to five of thirteen brokers; no cuts"): five analysts raised FY2027 EPS and EBITDA, the top of that range, with no new cut, but FY2026 EPS rose 0.8%, just short of the +1-2%. Against it: the reported figures are not in the sources read, and Morgan Stanley (30-Oct) forecast a conversion margin of 40.7%. Test: the reported conversion margin and organic gross profit

The SP led the estimates, then paused when they caught up: IMCD rose 4.3% from 26-Oct to 4-Nov with consensus flat, then fell 1.1% on the day consensus rose, as your 3-Nov market note expects ("Almost always, price leads estimates").

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

IMCD's Net Upgrade Breadth turned up on the results day on every line, after fading to zero through October. On the 5-Nov readings, FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth is +27% (5 raising, 1 cutting, of 15; +7% at 31-Oct), EBITDA +31% (5 raising, 1 cutting, of 13; 0%), revenue +21% (4 raising, 1 cutting, of 14; 0% from 28-Oct to 4-Nov) and PT +13% (2 raising, none cutting, of 15; 0% from 2 to 4-Nov). After the 29-Jul results PT breadth went from +13% to +47% in a month.

IMCD's average PT rose 1.1% to €117.3 on 5-Nov, its first move since August, and is 13.5% above the SP (11.2% on 3-Nov). Two analysts raised; the sources read do not name them. The gap remains narrow by the stock's own history: the last time it was near this (12.2%, April 2026 month-end), IMCD made its 4-May top. The named PTs in the sources read are Goldman Sachs €133 and Deutsche Bank €123 at the top, Morgan Stanley €110 (Equal-weight, maintained on 30-Oct, your weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026) and ING €108 (29-Jul, earnings review), with a Restricted Sell at €75 (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 3-Nov).

Ratings did not move on the results: 11 Buys, 3 others, 1 Sell since July. IMCD's ratings follow the gap to the PT more than the SP's momentum (14 Buys in autumn 2025 with the PT 56% to 65% above the SP; 9 by April 2026 with the PT 12% above the SP).

Where analysts are cutting: one analyst's cut to each of FY2027 EPS, EBITDA and revenue remains inside the 100-day window; the sources read do not name the broker. The stated caution on record is Morgan Stanley's (30-Oct): higher operating costs taking the third-quarter conversion margin to 40.7% against a Visible Alpha consensus of 42.7%, and leverage of 2.8 times EBITDA with few good targets restricting the acquisitions that have historically driven about half of IMCD's growth (AlphaSense research filed 3-Nov). Test: whether Morgan Stanley's €110 moves after 5-Nov.

1. SP and MAs

Hilton Food broke out of its five-week base on Wed 4-Nov, to 743.0p (+2.8%), and held it on Thu 5-Nov at 744.0p, its highest close since 2-Sep-2025. The breakout came on a Consumer staples rally day, and Hilton Food has risen less than its industry over the week and the month. Hilton Food is up 14.7% over 12 months (648.62p on 6-Nov-2025 to 744.0p) and 21.4% over 3 months (from 612.91p on 7-Aug). Over those 12 months Hilton Food fell 32.0% from 670.50p (28-Oct-2025) to 455.73p (29-Jan-2026), then rose 63.3% to 744.0p. Hilton Food's 5-session move, +4.0%, is 0.8 times its usual weekly move (4.8%); the Stoxx Europe 600's +0.9% explains 0.3 points of it (0.9% times a beta of 0.34), but Consumer staples rose 4.6%.

The legs of the latest advance: 12-Aug to 3-Sep, 604.92p to 707.90p, +17.0% (the 3-Sep first-half results, +12.2% on 6.61 times the 50D average volume); 3 to 11-Sep, to 666.41p, (5.9%); 11-Sep to 5-Nov, to 744.0p, +11.6% over 39 sessions. 3. RS

Hilton Food's sector and industry are now rising behind it: all three RS readings are at or near their highs of the year, for the first time in this advance.

  • Hilton Food's Stock RS: 90 on 5-Nov, from 87 at the 30-Oct month-end and 89 a month ago; 90 equals its highest reading in the 13-month series (9 and 23-Oct). It has read 81 or more at every Friday since 4-Sep, far above the Sell Criteria's lines of 70 and 50.
  • Food production's Sector RS: 68 on 5-Nov, from 55 at the 30-Oct reading and 58 on 3-Nov; its highest since 3-Apr (68), below the 31-Mar month-end's 74. It is 18 points above the line of 50.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS: 73 on 4 and 5-Nov, from 66 at the 30-Oct reading, equal to its 23-Oct reading, the highest in the 13 months the series holds. It is 43 points above the line of 30.
  • The order of the turn: Hilton Food's Stock RS turned first and alone (19 on 24-Jul to 84 on 4-Sep); the sector and industry followed from 9 to 16-Oct, dipped in the week to 29-Oct (Sector RS 48) and are rising again.

The industry's RS rose this week on days the Industrials fell, which reads as a rotation into staples rather than a general cut in investors' exposure. On 4-Nov Consumer staples rose 2.5% while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% and Industrials and capital goods fell 0.3%; on 5-Nov staples rose 0.3% against the market's (0.4%) and Industrials' (0.9%). The Industry Digest's test of 30-Oct is half passed: Consumer staples' Industry RS above 50 at both the 30-Oct and 6-Nov Friday readings would support a rotation, and 73 today makes the 6-Nov reading likely to pass.

Precedent: Food production's Sector RS has crossed 50 and fallen back three times this year, the longest spell six Fridays (6-Mar to 10-Apr, peaking at 74 at the 31-Mar month-end); the current spell is three Fridays. Test, Fri 6-Nov: a fourth Friday above 50 is also Hilton Food's second Friday in the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection.

4. Consensus by named FY

Hilton Food's EPS, revenue and PT consensus have not moved since 8-Oct; the only change, a rise of 2.6% to 2.9% in EBITDA for every year on 5-Nov, came without any change to EPS, so it reads as a change of basis by one analyst, not an upgrade. Hilton Food's financial year ends in December; figures are in sterling.

  • EPS over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2026 52.2p, +1.5%, +6.9%, +9.7%; FY2027 56.9p, +1.0%, +6.1%, +8.1%; FY2028 62.1p, (0.7%), +2.9%, +3.9%. Over 12 months each is still down 17% to 19%, after the November 2025 profit warning.
  • Implied EPS growth: FY2027 over FY2026 +9.0%, FY2028 over FY2027 +9.1%, from +9.8% and +12.5% at the July month-end: analysts have kept the FY2027 step and trimmed the FY2028 step, which is pulling earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory.
  • EBITDA on 5-Nov: FY2026 £145.7m to £149.8m (+2.9%, on the unrounded £145.65m and £149.84m), FY2027 £156.4m to £160.5m (+2.6%), FY2028 £163.9m to £168.2m (+2.6%), with the count of analysts unchanged at 5 and EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth unchanged. A rise of about £4m in the mean of five analysts' forecasts equals about £20m (five times £4m) in one analyst's forecast; with EPS unchanged, that fits a move to lease-inclusive (IFRS 16) EBITDA, the basis on which the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's model has FY2026 at £167.6m. The cause is not in the sources read.
  • Implied FY2027 EBITDA margin: 3.34%, from 3.26% before 5-Nov and 3.35% at the July month-end; revenue rises with beef prices that pass through Hilton Food's cost-plus contracts at cost (earnings review), so the margin is not a profit signal.

The SP has run ahead of the estimates and keeps doing so: over 3 months Hilton Food's SP is up 21.4% and FY2027 EPS 6.1%. The A&J Memo (written 3-Nov, dated 4-Nov on the dashboard) puts FY2027 consensus about 4% above its model (FY2027 EPS 56p, flat on FY2026) and expects a confirmation at the January 2027 trading update, then a 3% to 6% cut to FY2027 consensus after the 7-Apr-2027 results, led by Deutsche Bank.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Q — Risk from Tesco and CEE?". Tesco is the nearer risk, as a slow loss of streams rather than of the contract: the A&J Memo records Tesco's contract extended in early 2026, but Tesco moving its Scottish beef stream to Foyle and Kepak in the first half of FY2026, and models about 1% of West volume lost a year; it puts a material anchor-contract loss within three years at about 3% to 5%. Central and Eastern Europe is a growth source, not yet a risk: Polish fresh prepared food volumes grew 26% in the first half of 2026, with up to £30m of Polish capacity planned across 2026 and 2027 (Sector Digest, 15-Oct) and a capacity decision around the end of 2026.

On 28 to 31-Oct you also wrote: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". The next dated leg is the January 2027 trading update (Christmas trading and Canada's commissioning); the Saudi joint venture (fourth quarter of 2026) and the Polish decision come before it but are small in profit.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings follow its SP: 4 Buys of 4 at a 14-month high, as at the 2025 top. Hilton Food has had 4 Buys and no other rating since the 31-Oct month-end; it had 2 Buys and 2 other at the July and August month-ends, before the 3-Sep results, and 5 Buys and 1 other at the May 2025 month-end, the month of its 905.1p high, before a 49.6% fall. The A&J Memo reads it as a risk: "unanimous Buys and thin liquidity".

Hilton Food's average PT (795p since 8-Oct) is now 6.9% above the SP, from 10.0% on 3-Nov, as the SP rises into an unchanged PT. The gap was 3.9% at the 30-Sep month-end, the narrowest since at least August 2024, and the SP then fell 3.3% in three sessions (718.9p to 694.9p on 5-Oct) and based for five weeks. Named PTs: Deutsche Bank 850p from 815p, Buy (3-Sep, earnings review); Berenberg 810p from 790p (7-Sep, as read for the 28-Oct note); Peel Hunt 720p, which the A&J Memo notes "is at the price". At 744.0p the SP is above Peel Hunt's PT. Test: PT raises that follow the SP would show the sell-side extending the case; the SP reaching the average PT with no raise would repeat the 30-Sep set-up that preceded the five-week base.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure and unchanged since 31-Oct; no new raise has arrived since September's.

  • FY2027 EPS +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5); FY2027 EBITDA +60% (3 raising, none cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, none cutting); PT +50% (2 raising, none cutting, of 4). At the 30-Sep month-end the same measures read +50%, +83%, +50% and +60%. The 5-Nov EBITDA rise did not change the EBITDA count (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • Where an analyst is cutting: one of Hilton Food's five analysts has cut FY2027 EPS, a cut carried since the 30-Sep month-end. The sources read do not name the broker. The stated logic for lower EPS in the sources sits below EBITDA: Deutsche Bank's post-results forecasts "incorporating updated foreign exchange rates and borrowing costs" (AlphaSense research of 2-Nov, as read for the 2-Nov note), and the A&J Memo's finance-charge step-up of about £3.5m in FY2027.

Precedent: at the April 2026 turn the breadth led the SP by eleven weeks; now the SP leads and the breadth fades. Test: new raises after the January 2027 trading update, or the first PT cut since the March 2026 month-end.

Consumer staples rose 10.7% over the month, twice Hilton Food's move.

  • Sell-side estimates: Bell Food Group's 3M EPS revision is +10%, Hilton Food's +6%, Greencore's +3%, Cranswick's +1% and LDC's (1%). On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Staples RS really accelerating but still a very low P/E. Especially beverages, food production, food retail". Inside Food production the acceleration is going to the large laggards, not to Hilton Food. Over the month Barry Callebaut's Stock RS rose from 17 to 69 (its full-year results were due on 4-Nov, per the Sector Digest; it rose 14.0% over the week), Nestle's from 36 to 67, Danone's from 9 to 39 and Emmi's from 57 to 82. Which group Hilton Food trades with now: the UK food makers. Associated British Foods, the stock outside the cohort whose daily moves track Hilton Food's most closely (average correlation 0.37), rose 6.4% on 4-Nov, the day Hilton Food broke out (its volume is in item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)); the cause is not in the sources read. On 3-Nov Hilton Food fell with Greencore, Cranswick and Associated British Foods while the continental peers rose.

What the peers imply: Hilton Food is a leader at its high while slower names catch up; if the staples rotation fades, the precedent is 1-Oct, when the cohort fell 1.6% and Hilton Food 2.4%. Test: Hilton Food holding above 726.4p on days the laggards rise shows money rotating without leaving the leader.
