  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly present: the 4-Nov breakout came on 1.14x the 50D average volume; the 5-Nov extension to €187.0 reversed to the low on 1.49x, but the close held above €180.4.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: no longer: it crossed the absolute 200D five times from 13-Oct to 2-Nov and now trades 5.2% above it.
  • Verdict: neither a retest nor a broken trend, but a base whose upper edge is being tested. The 28-Oct pullback to the rising 150D MA held on the lightest volume in 25 sessions and the lower highs are broken, but two MA-order tests fail and the breakout volume was ordinary. What would settle it: a close above €184.3 on 1.5x the 50D average or more confirms a breakout; a close back below €180.4 on above-average volume makes 4-Nov a failed breakout, the sixth in the zone this year.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: the 31-Aug and 18-Sep index-review and option-expiry days sit inside D'Ieteren's 50D average and lower its volume ratios by about 3%; the ratios below leave them out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's in brackets.

D'Ieteren's breakout came on ordinary volume and the next session's selling on more: the last two sessions are a warning, not a confirmation.

  • 4-Nov: +3.3% to €182.8, at 0.88 of the day's range, on 68,663 shares (€12.6m), 1.14x (1.11x) the 50D average: D'Ieteren's highest close since 7-Aug (€183.3).
  • 5-Nov: D'Ieteren traded up to €187.0, its highest intraday price since 17-Apr, and closed at the day's low, €182.3, (0.3%), on 91,921 shares (€16.8m), 1.49x (1.46x). It was its heaviest volume since 15-Oct and its first down day at 1.4x or more since the 24-Sep low; Consumer discretionary rose 0.1%.
  • Inference: sellers outweighed buyers across the two sessions, in the zone where D'Ieteren's rallies have stalled since April (item 1 (SP and MAs)).

The heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) of the last 25 sessions are still the four of 6 to 15-Oct: 6-Oct (+0.9% on 1.61x (1.55x)), 8-Oct (+2.2% on 2.72x (2.61x), €22.9m, the Belron banks report), 9-Oct (+2.5% on 1.59x (1.54x)) and 15-Oct (+0.1% on 1.87x (1.81x), €17.7m, closing at the day's low against an industry up 1.6%). 5-Nov, just short of the line, repeats 15-Oct: a close at the low on the fortnight's heaviest volume against a flat-to-rising industry. After 15-Oct D'Ieteren drifted for ten sessions and fell 3.1% from 21 to 28-Oct.

Distribution and accumulation days: the volume script counts 3 distribution days (7-Oct, 14-Oct, 5-Nov) and 4 accumulation days (6, 8, 9, 15-Oct) in the last 25 sessions: the accumulation is a month old, and the newest distribution day sits at the top of the move. The dashboard reads 1; it has not counted 7 and 14-Oct, which fit its own definition (a data question, as on 3-Nov).

Up against down volume: the dashboard's 20-session ratio (average up-day volume over average down-day volume) fell from 1.16 on 3-Nov to 0.92, and its 5-session ratio reads 0.82: D'Ieteren's average down day now trades more than its average up day. The volume script reproduces the 0.92 from the chart file; total volume on up days against down days reads 1.18 over 20 sessions and 1.08 over 5 only because up days outnumber down days (11 to 9, and 3 to 2), so there is no data question. On either basis the ratio fell during the push to the high. Over the advance from 24-Sep the script reads 1.10 (4 heavy up days, none down), between the June to August advance (1.02) and the March to April one (1.61), both of which gave back 13% to 15%.

The trend of volume: 79,519 shares a day from 6 to 16-Oct, 42,825 on the 22 to 30-Oct pullback and 67,578 from 2 to 5-Nov, against a 50D average of 63,124. Volume dried up on the pullback and returned on the push; that the push's heaviest session was a down day is the flaw.

Volume at the MAs: On 28-Oct D'Ieteren closed 0.5% below its rising 150D MA and just below its 50D MA, on 0.51x the 50D average volume, the lightest of the 25 sessions. It regained both MAs on 29 and 30-Oct on 0.56x and 0.65x, and the absolute 200D MA on 2-Nov on 0.91x. Sellers were absent, but buyers did not commit until 4-Nov. Precedent: the decline to 24-Sep ended with its first heavy up days in the eighth and tenth sessions after the low, 6-Oct (+0.9% on 1.61x) and 8-Oct (2.72x); the last breakout attempt, 4-Sep (+3.4% to €180.4, 1.32x, dashboard), reversed the next session into an 11.3% fall.

Relative price confirmed the break of the September and October highs, not the August high. It reached 99.43 on 5-Nov, its highest in 63 sessions, above its 4-Sep (98.85) and 22-Oct (98.11) highs but below 101.10 (5-Aug). It is 15.3% below its 52W high (2-Feb), from 18.5% at 30-Oct, and 0.5% below its relative 200D MA, below which it has stayed since 6-Aug.

Peers' volume: on 4-Nov Auto1 rose 3.2% on 3.03x and ZIGUP 0.8% on 2.35x; on 5-Nov Inchcape traded 2.87x at (0.1%), Auto1 rose 1.0% on 1.49x and ZIGUP 0.1% on 1.44x, while Sixt fell 1.2% on 1.26x. Of the sector stocks that fell on 5-Nov, only Sixt fell on above-average volume; Inter Cars' 4.2% fall came on 0.94x. Inference: 5-Nov's heavy selling near the high was mostly D'Ieteren's own, shared only with Sixt, which weighs for the first hypothesis in item 1 (SP and MAs), the range's supply, over sector profit-taking.

Would confirm the move continues: a close above €184.3 (the 5-Aug close) on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 92,300 shares on this item's basis without the mechanical-volume days, 94,700 on the dashboard's; about €17m), with relative price above its relative 200D MA. Would confirm a turn: a close back below €180.4 on above-average volume, making 4-Nov a failed breakout.

3. RS

D'Ieteren's three RS readings stand on or above the lower lines the Sell Criteria read (Stock RS 50, Sector RS 50, Industry RS 30) for the first time since 4-Sep, with Stock RS still 20 points below its 70 line; the stock and its sector turned first, the industry followed on 4-Nov.

  • Stock RS: 50 on 5-Nov, from 45 on 3-Nov and 31 on 29-Oct; 36 a month ago, 49 three months ago, 22 twelve months ago. It is the highest since 56 on 4-Sep, and exactly on the 50 line of the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50".
  • Sector RS (Automotive - retail, aftermarket, leasing and rental): 58, from 48 on 3-Nov and a 12-month low of 29 on 29-Oct; 36 a month ago, 66 three months ago. The highest since 11-Sep, 8 points above the 50 line.
  • Industry RS (Consumer discretionary): 46, from 40 on 4-Nov and 26 on 3-Nov (26 also at every Friday from 9 to 30-Oct); the highest since 14-Aug. Consumer discretionary rose 3.7% in the week, third of 16 industries (Market chart).

The order of the turn: sector and stock from 29-Oct, industry from 4-Nov. Inference: a rotation into Consumer discretionary now adds to the sector rebound that lifted D'Ieteren first; it is the part D'Ieteren cannot influence.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's autumn RS rallies faded (Stock RS 56 on 4-Sep to 34 by 30-Sep; 47 on 9-Oct to 31 by 29-Oct). What differs: Stock RS 50 is the first higher RS high since September, made with the SP's break of its lower highs. From its two earlier lows Stock RS led the +37.0% and +16.0% advances (14 on 12-Dec-2025 to 61 by 23-Jan-2026; 29 at the 8-Jun low to 56 by 1-Jul).

Would confirm the turn: Stock RS and Sector RS above 50 at the Friday readings of 6 and 13-Nov. Would refute it: Stock RS back below 47 with relative price below 98.1 (the 22-Oct high).

4. Consensus by named FY

D'Ieteren's consensus is unchanged since 27-Oct: the SP rose 6.4% in the week on estimates cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months. Price is leading, and no estimate has followed.

  • EPS, over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2026 (year to Dec-26) €12.75, (2.4%), (4.8%), (6.3%); FY2027 €15.09, (1.7%), (3.5%), (4.8%); FY2028 €17.38, (1.9%), (3.8%), (3.5%).
  • Revenue and EBITDA: unchanged since 30-Sep, after 3-month cuts of (1.5%) to (2.4%) and (3.3%) to (5.9%) following the 9-Sep results.
  • The trajectory: analysts cut the nearer year fastest and push the recovery out. Implied EPS growth FY2026 to FY2027 is 18.4% (16.7% three months ago); the implied EBITDA margin fell in every year (FY2027 8.3%, from 8.5%).

The consolidated revenue and EBITDA lines exclude Belron. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 3-Nov says to read D'Ieteren "on the proportionate group-share basis, never on FactSet's consolidated lines": the consolidated perimeter is D'Ieteren Automotive, Parts Holding Europe and Moleskine, while Belron (50.3%) and TVH (40%) are equity-accounted. So the revenue cuts and the (56%) revenue Net Upgrade Breadth that the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50" reads measure mainly D'Ieteren Automotive; EPS carries Belron and is cut too.

Your 3-Nov rules. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". D'Ieteren fails it on every FY. The same note says "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)", and D'Ieteren's 13.9% rise from 24-Sep, as Sector RS rose from its 12-month low, fits that lead. Against: the A&J Memo expects FY2027 consensus to be cut 2 to 4% after a cautious framing at the 10-Mar-2027 results. Test: the 30-Nov month-end breadth for EPS and revenue.

  • For your ruling: the upgrades rule is not in the Position Entry and Exit Rules, so for an Early Innings Probing Bet it neither sells nor blocks an add; whether it applies to D'Ieteren is yours.

On your investor relations call. On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "D'Ieteren is up from 165 to 175 L1M, and up 5% in Nov-26. My concern re. a weak IR call still remains". D'Ieteren is now up 5.4% in November on closes (€172.9 to €182.3), so your 5% holds. The 15-Oct call's content is still not filed. After it came a close at the low on 1.87x (15-Oct), a PT step down (16-Oct) and a 1.7% FY2027 EPS cut (27-Oct). Hypothesis: the 5-Nov close at the low on 1.49x is the second time in three weeks that sellers met D'Ieteren on a strong day, consistent with holders sharing your concern; no source read says so.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's analysts rate it Buy unanimously while every estimate breadth is negative: the ratings and PTs value Belron, the estimates track D'Ieteren Automotive. Nothing changed on 4 or 5-Nov.

  • Ratings: 14 Buys of 14, from 12 at 31-Aug and 6 of 7 twenty-four months ago: coverage has doubled. Every analyst has rated D'Ieteren Buy at every month-end since January 2026, through the 21.3% and 13.2% falls, so the ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum.
  • Average PT: €234.7, (2.0%) since 1-Oct, after unnamed steps down on 16 and 27-Oct. Its gap to the SP has narrowed to 28.7%, from 46.9% at 30-Sep, by the SP rising; in 2026 the gap has run from 12.6% (10-Feb high) to 51.4% (30-Mar low) with the PT between €220 and €240, so it moves with the SP alone. The latest named changes were raises on a higher Belron value: KBC Securities to €250 (16-Sep) and ING to €263 (28-Sep).

Net Upgrade Breadth on 5-Nov: FY2027 EPS (21%) (1 raising, 4 cutting, of 14), from +9% at 31-Aug; EBITDA (62%) (0 and 5, of 8); revenue (56%) (0 and 5, of 9); PT +29% (5 and 1, of 14), easing from +46% at 30-Sep. No analyst has raised revenue since the March month-end or EBITDA since May. PT breadth against estimate breadth is the case in one line: the PTs carry a Belron listing value, the estimates D'Ieteren Automotive's decline.

Where analysts are cutting: D'Ieteren Automotive. Deutsche Bank cut its profit before tax forecasts 1% after the 9-Sep results "to reflect the heavier-than-expected drag from Automotive retail operations", and brokers model the Automotive margin at 2.1% against 3.5% or more before (earnings review, 18-Sep). Jefferies expects Belgian fleet demand to "taper from 2027 onward" as 100% tax deductibility for corporate electric vehicles ends (Jefferies, 15-Oct). The A&J Memo records the widest dispute on the same business: Bernstein's 1.7% FY2028 Automotive margin against Deutsche Bank's near 4%. October's cutters are not named in the sources read.

Precedent: the same split ran at the March and April 2026 month-ends (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%)) across the 20.9% rally to 17-Apr, most of which D'Ieteren gave back by 8-Jun. Test: PT cuts without Belron news would mean the Belron value is being cut; an EPS raise would be the first estimate support for the SP.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's (the page's peer table); the AlphaSense report of 4-Nov covers none of them.

Since 24-Sep D'Ieteren has traded with the parts and rental half of its sector, and on 5-Nov it reversed with that half's leaders.

  • SP technicals: from 24-Sep to 5-Nov D'Ieteren rose 13.9%, with Inter Cars (+14.3%), Sixt (+10.4%) and ZIGUP (+9.2%), against Ayvens ((4.5%)) and Inchcape ((17.7%)). On 5-Nov Inter Cars fell 4.2% at its low after +4.8% on 4-Nov, and Sixt fell 1.2% near its low on 1.26x, while Ayvens (+1.9%), Bilia (+1.4%) and Auto1 (+1.0%) rose.
  • Sell-side estimates: D'Ieteren's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((56%)) and 3-month EPS revision ((4%)) sit with the broken half (Ayvens (67%), Auto1 (64%)), against positive breadth at Sixt, Inter Cars, ZIGUP and Bilia (+14% to +33%). D'Ieteren's SP trades with the leaders and its estimates with the laggards; item 4 (Consensus by named FY) traces that gap to D'Ieteren Automotive.
  • RS: Sixt's Stock RS rose from 39 to 62 in a month, the sector's clearest turn; Ayvens (18), Auto1 (9) and Inchcape (6) stay at the bottom, so D'Ieteren's Sector RS of 58 rests on a broad sector rebound, not on the laggards recovering.
  • Newsflow, guidance and communications: nothing new in the sources read since 3-Nov. The warnings remain in new-car retail (Inchcape cut its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance to about 5.5% on 22-Oct (Jefferies, 22-Oct)), D'Ieteren Automotive's business.

Who turned first: Sixt's Stock RS in October, then D'Ieteren's from 29-Oct. Sixt is also the most correlated stock outside the cohort (0.25, weak); the A&J Memo finds the sector explains only 19% of D'Ieteren's daily variance over six months, so peers set its tone on days without company news, not its direction.

What the peers imply: with three sector stocks at Stock RS 18 or below, a reversal led by Inter Cars and Sixt would take D'Ieteren's Sector RS back towards 50 by the 30-Nov month-end. Sixt's third-quarter results on 12-Nov are the dated test: a beat extending the rental turn supports D'Ieteren's Sector RS; a miss would show whether 5-Nov began the sector's profit-taking.

What the rules say for D'Ieteren now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • A breakout on closes, not on volume: the 4-Nov close above €180.4 came on 1.14x on a market-led day, and 5-Nov reversed to the low on 1.49x, in a zone that has capped five rallies this year.
  • RS has turned, led by the sector: Stock RS 50, Sector RS 58 and Industry RS 46 stand on or above their lower Sell Criteria lines (50, 50 and 30) for the first time since 4-Sep; Stock RS is still below 70.
  • Estimates have not followed: EPS is cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months and no analyst has raised revenue since March, so D'Ieteren still fails your 3-Nov upgrades rule.

Tranche 1 (2.62% now, 2.5% at entry, bought 12-Aug, on the 50D MA): clear. 50D MA €170.67, stop-loss €160.43, close €182.3: a 6.4% fall reaches the MA, 12.0% the stop-loss. Your 2-Nov-2026 ruling cleared its 3-Sep confirmation (kept at the 30-Oct close); no close since 2-Nov has been at or below the MA.

The nearest sell tests:

  • The 10-day stop-loss rule is nearest, and moving away: the close stood 6.8% above the 50D MA on 5-Nov, 0.5% above a week earlier and 4.0% below four weeks earlier (8-Oct). A 6.4% fall is 1.7 usual weekly moves; at a flat SP the rising MA narrows it to about 4.6% by 30-Nov.
  • A test on ordinary volume confirms on day 2 (the volume after day 1 averaging 0.75x the 50D average): every session since 2-Nov traded at 0.88x or more, so a first close at or below the MA would likely confirm the next session and sell at the following Tuesday.
  • About €160 to €161.5 brings three lines together: the stop-loss (€160.43), the 24-Sep low (€160.0) and relative price 25% below its 52W high (€161.45 at a flat market, read at 30-Nov). The MA test would act long before.
  • The six Sell Criteria, information only: at today's reading, Fri 6-Nov, the next reading day, Industry RS (46) will probably read above 30: Consumer discretionary's Industry RS moves in steps of about 7 points (26, 33, 40, 46), so a reading below 30 needs a fall of three steps, back to 26, in one session (it rose two steps, from 26 to 40, on 4-Nov), and the relative 200D MA will probably read as falling again (about (0.80%) at a flat market). At 30-Nov, Stock RS, exactly 50, decides the Net Upgrade Breadth test: 49 or below would give a sell signal for a Core Investment.

The record of a comparable setup: on 6-Aug, thirteen weeks before 5-Nov, D'Ieteren also closed 6.6% above its 50D MA (€183.5); it closed below the MA on 17-Aug, three sessions after tranche 1 was bought, inside the 13.2% decline from 5-Aug to 24-Sep.

What decides the next step:

  • The rules allow an add: tranche 1 is clear, D'Ieteren holds one tranche with 3.38% of room below its 6% limit on current value; the next tranche would be 3.0% (0.5% of capital at a stop-loss 12.0% below, capped at 3%). Consumer discretionary holdings total 4.50% of capital, far inside the 30% cap.
  • Where D'Ieteren stands for an add: on the Master Dashboard's tests, none of the three; on this note's judgement, the second stage, may be forming a volatility contraction pattern. The Master Dashboard reads Pulling Back within an uptrend 2 of 4, Basing 2 of 4, Healthy Retest 3 of 6 (on the 50D MA, the SP 6.8% above it) and a volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4, because its pattern test checks only for a Stage 2 uptrend, which fails with the 150D MA below the absolute 200D MA. This note's judgement moves D'Ieteren one stage above the 3-Nov note's basing: the declines in its nine-month range have contracted (21.3%, 14.6%, 13.2%) on rising lows, and the 21 to 28-Oct pullback inside the advance from 24-Sep was only 3.1%. Against: a volatility contraction pattern normally forms inside a Stage 2 uptrend, and D'Ieteren's MAs stand in the reverse of that order until about 24-Nov at a flat SP. It is not the third stage, breaking out: the Position Entry and Exit Rules name a VCP breakout on volume above the 50-day average as supporting evidence for an add, and the 4-Nov close above €180.4 came on 1.14x, what item 2 (Volume) calls ordinary volume, before 5-Nov reversed to the low on more (1.49x); two MA-order tests fail and relative price is below its relative 200D MA.
  • Add triggers: a positive retest of a rising MA partly present; a broadening of the case partly present (item 12 (adding capital)). In the "NTS at 03-Nov 1600h" block of your note you asked for "More dynamic up-sizing of probing bets when they break out": on volume and the 5-Nov reversal, D'Ieteren's breakout is not yet one to size up on.
  • Dates: Sixt's third-quarter results, 12-Nov; the 30-Nov month-end; Eric Machiels chief executive from December 2026; FY2026 results, 10-Mar-2027 (confirmed); Belron listing news at any time.
  • What would change this reading: a close above €184.3 on 1.5x the 50D average (about 94,700 shares) moves D'Ieteren to the third stage, breaking out; a close back below €180.4 on above-average volume returns it to basing; a close at or below €170.67 starts the 10-day test and blocks an add while it runs.

Richard decides.

1. SP and MAs

Carlsberg followed through on its breakout: its SP rose 3.1% in the two sessions since the previous run, to a 52W closing high of DKK 1,031.5 on 5-Nov, 5.1% above the DKK 981.8 ceiling of 12-Feb, but the follow-through was the group's, not Carlsberg's. Carlsberg is up 10.4% over 5 sessions against 0.9% for the Stoxx Europe 600; after the 0.8 points that Carlsberg's beta of 0.87 to the index explains, 9.6 points of the move are Carlsberg's own against the index (a figure that still includes the Beverages group's rise), and those 9.6 points are 2.9 times Carlsberg's usual weekly move of 3.3%; it is up 18.2% over 20 sessions, 11.6% over 3 months and 38.8% over 12.

Carlsberg's three phases since its 22-Oct high, against the cohort brewers and Consumer staples:

  • 22 to 29-Oct, the pullback: (4.7%) to DKK 934.2: the fall was Carlsberg's own and AB InBev's ((6.8%)), on their third-quarter statements and China, while Heineken held ((0.1%)).
  • 29-Oct to 2-Nov, the breakout: +7.6% against +4.7% to +5.0% for the other brewers and +2.2% for Consumer staples: about 3 points of Carlsberg's gain were its own, beyond the other brewers' move.
  • 3 to 5-Nov, the follow-through: +3.1% against Heineken +3.9%, AB InBev +3.7%, Royal Unibrew +2.1%, Consumer staples +2.8% and the Stoxx Europe 600 +1.4%, while Industrials and capital goods fell 1.2% and Technology 2.0%: the advance was the Beverages group's and the rotation's, not Carlsberg's own.

Developments. Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run; I used his report of 4-Nov-2026, and no source read covers Carlsberg on 4 or 5-Nov.

  • 28 and 29-Oct, the third-quarter statement: organic revenue beat (+3.0% against 2.6%), volume missed (+0.3% against 0.6%), China volume (10%) (Morgan Stanley, Deutsche Bank, 29-Oct); guidance reiterated. The SP fell 3.0%.
  • 30-Oct, management's London sell-side meeting (Bernstein, 30-Oct): pricing can "fully offset" 2027 cost inflation; Carlsberg rose 3.9% on 2.43x volume: the reassurance explains the reversal.
  • 4 and 5-Nov: the cause of Carlsberg's moves is not in the sources read; they match the staples rotation. On 5-Nov Coca-Cola HBC fell 17.9% on a Russian threat to its subsidiaries (item 9 (Peers)).

Hypotheses for the advance since 29-Oct, ranked by evidence. 1. The defensive rotation is carrying Beverages, and Carlsberg leads it. For: from 29-Oct to 5-Nov Consumer staples rose 4.6% against 0.9% for the Stoxx Europe 600 and (5.3%) for Technology; Beverages' leaders closed at or near 52W highs on 5-Nov. Against: about 3 points of Carlsberg's advance were its own, on 30-Oct and 2-Nov, beyond the other brewers' move. 2. The statement removed an overhang and the sell-side turned. For: the heavy 30-Oct reversal, PT raises, breadth positive on every measure. Against: estimates up under 1%, on currency; no Carlsberg-specific gain since 2-Nov. Test: a group-wide pullback that Carlsberg matches favours the first; Carlsberg holding DKK 981.8 while the brewers fall favours the second.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "[+] Carlsberg breaking out to 1000 DKK. Technically 52W high, even though it's been trading sideways in choppy manner". Confirmed, with follow-through: two more 52W closing highs, DKK 1,026.0 and 1,031.5, on average volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

And: "SP has popped hard like L2W in Feb-26 and Jul-26 (both times after earnings…?) - chances are it retraces somewhat here". Over 20 sessions this pop now equals February's; over 10 it is far less vertical. Carlsberg rose 18.2% in the 20 sessions to 5-Nov against 17.8% to the 12-Feb high (July: 4.3%), but 5.2% in the last 10 against 19.2%; it closes 14.6% above its 50D MA against 19.0% at February's high and 8.9% at July's. The retrace is not yet in evidence: no down day since 3-Nov.

The MAs are now almost in an advance's order: 20D (DKK 955.74), 100D (906.06), 50D (900.11), 150D (885.20), absolute 200D (883.79). The 150D MA crossed above the absolute 200D MA on 3-Nov; the 50D MA is rising fastest of the long MAs (+1.4% over 5 sessions) and at this pace crosses the 100D within about a week.

Carlsberg against your trend-health tests (no pullback yet to judge): all five pass: the absolute 200D MA rising (+1.7% over 20 sessions); the 150D MA above it (by DKK 1.41); the 50D MA above both; the SP above every MA (16.7% above the absolute 200D); at the 52W high, 40.8% above its low. Stage 3 flags present: the largest one-day and one-week falls since the advance began (29-Oct's (3.0%); 22 to 29-Oct's (4.7%)), both on mixed results news; wider swings (an average daily move of 2.2% over 5 sessions against 1.5% over 20). Absent: lower lows, more down days than up (22 up, 12 down since 18-Sep), a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, the SP tracking a rising MA. Verdict: a healthy advance at a new high, extended above its MAs; the volume tests at the end of item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high) would settle it.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's new 52W highs of 4 and 5-Nov came on average volume, not heavy buying: the breakout still rests on one heavy reversal day (30-Oct), and on 5-Nov the peers traded more heavily than Carlsberg did. On 4-Nov Carlsberg rose 2.5% on 1.03x its 50D average volume (246,134 shares, DKK 252.5m), closing at 0.94 of the day's range; on 5-Nov it rose 0.5% on 1.06x (251,973 shares, DKK 259.9m), traded to a new intraday high of DKK 1,040.0 and closed at 0.72 of the range. On 5-Nov AB InBev traded 1.74x its 50D average, Heineken 1.59x and Diageo 1.88x.

Carlsberg's heavy-volume days in the last 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more; dashboard basis, the figure without the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep in brackets):

  • 15-Oct, accumulation: +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x), 321,386 shares, DKK 299.9m, a 1.6% gap up, a close at 0.89 of the range; the move was the group's (Royal Unibrew also +3.6%).
  • 27-Oct, distribution: (1.4%) on 2.07x (2.14x), 466,730 shares, DKK 445.1m, a close at 0.11 of the range, the session before the 28-Oct third-quarter statement; Royal Unibrew (0.3%) on 0.8x and Heineken (0.6%) on 1.6x, so the selling was mostly Carlsberg's own.
  • 30-Oct, accumulation: +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), 567,548 shares, DKK 551.1m, a 2.2% gap up and a close at 0.59 of the range, on the day of management's London sell-side meeting (item 1 (SP and MAs)); Carlsberg's heaviest up-day volume since 25-Nov-2024.

Distribution against accumulation, 25 sessions: 2 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct, around the statement) against 6 accumulation days (7, 14, 15, 28, 30-Oct, 2-Nov) on the Assistant Portfolio Manager's own count, made from each day's SP change and its volume against the 50D average; the dashboard counts 1 distribution day, from 7 in mid-September.

Up-day against down-day volume (average up-day volume over average down-day volume, the dashboard's basis): 1.11 over 20 sessions (the same without mechanical-volume days) and 1.15 over the dashboard's 5-session window, which leaves out 30-Oct (1.46 with it). Over the whole advance from 18-Sep (+22.7%, 34 sessions) it is 1.06, with 2 heavy up days and 1 heavy down day. That is still the weak point: the September 2025 to February 2026 advance ran at 1.26 (9 heavy up days) and March to May 2026 at 1.16 (3); only June to July 2026 (1.02), which gave way to the 12.9% fall, was thinner.

Against that, participation is rising. The volume trend (20D against 50D average volume) is 1.27x, and the 50D average value traded has risen 19%, from DKK 180.8m on 2-Oct to DKK 215.1m: more money is trading Carlsberg at higher prices, even if no single day since 30-Oct has been heavy. On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The rise to 970 is on very thin volume - negative for sustaining it IMHO". Partly answered since: the move through DKK 970 came on 30-Oct's 2.43x, but the move from DKK 1,000 to 1,031.5 came on volume close to average.

Precedent: of Carlsberg's last three lows, only 24-Mar turned on heavy up days (31-Mar on 1.9x, 8-Apr on 1.5x); 4-Jun and 18-Sep turned quietly (1.3x at most in ten sessions), which is why this advance's up/down ratio is thin.

Carlsberg's relative price is closing on its 12-Feb high but has not confirmed the SP's 52W high. Relative price against the Stoxx Europe 600 is 4.6% below its 52W high of 12-Feb (6.5% on 3-Nov, 12.8% on 29-Oct), while the SP closed at its own 52W high. Relative price needs about 4.8% more outperformance against the Stoxx Europe 600 to confirm the SP's high; it is 10.2% above its relative 200D MA.

Confirms the breakout: a close above DKK 1,040.0 on 1.5x or more, closing in the top third of the range, or a pullback towards DKK 981.8 (4.8% below the 5-Nov close) on volume below the 50D average that holds. Confirms a failure: a close back below DKK 981.8 on above-average volume, then below the 29-Oct low of DKK 934.2 (9.4% below).

3. RS

Carlsberg's Stock RS reached 81 on 5-Nov, the highest since the series starts in October 2025, and all three RS readings sit in the 70 to 80s together, as they did at the Fri 23-Oct reading (73, 85 and 73). Each RS is a percentile rank from 0 to 99.

  • Carlsberg's Stock RS: 48 at the Fri 2-Oct reading, 54 at Fri 9-Oct, 73 at Fri 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct (the statement day), 70 at the Fri 30-Oct month-end, 76 on 4-Nov and 81 on 5-Nov. It was 22 at the 31-Oct-2025 month-end.
  • Beverages' Sector RS: 80, from 82 on 4-Nov, 76 at 30-Oct, 85 at 23-Oct and 50 at 2-Oct. It fell 2 points on 5-Nov, the day Coca-Cola HBC fell 17.9% (item 9 (Peers)).
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS: 73 on 4 and 5-Nov, equal to its 23-Oct reading, a 13-month high (Industry Digest, 30-Oct), from 66 at 30-Oct. The Industry Digest's test of a durable rotation (Industry RS above 50 at the 30-Oct and 6-Nov Fridays; staples rising when Industrials fall) is passing.
  • The order of the turn: Carlsberg's Stock RS and Beverages' Sector RS turned together at the Fri 25-Sep reading, Consumer staples' Industry RS from 9-Oct; Beverages led to 23-Oct, Carlsberg since the 29-Oct statement.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Very solid looking Stock, Sector and Industry RS of 70-80 now". Now confirmed in full: 81, 80 and 73.

Precedent: Carlsberg's Stock RS topped at 74 (Fri 13-Feb) and 68 (31-Jul month-end), then fell to 42 (20-Mar) and 41 (18-Sep) with 20.0% and 12.9% SP falls. At 81 it is above both, which weakens the reading that the 70s are Carlsberg's ceiling. How fast it can fall: Carlsberg's largest Stock RS fall within 25 days is 22 points (1 to 15-May-2026); Beverages' Sector RS fell 38 points in 12 days (31-Jul to 12-Aug). Refuted as a top if Carlsberg's Stock RS holds 70 or more at the Fri 13-Nov and Fri 20-Nov readings with the SP above DKK 981.8.

Peers' Stock RS (peer table): Heineken 76 (41 a month ago), AB InBev 74 (65), Royal Unibrew 30 (10); in Beverages Coca-Cola Europacific Partners 82 and Rémy Cointreau 81. Carlsberg leads its cohort.

4. Consensus by named FY

Carlsberg's consensus is still rising at the margin, later years slightly faster, but the rise remains mostly translation currency and has not yet undone the cuts of the last three to six months.

  • The chart's 1, 3 and 6-month changes to 5-Nov: FY2026 EPS DKK 65.18, +0.4%, +0.6% and +0.9%; FY2027 EPS DKK 70.40, +0.8%, (0.2%) and (0.6%); FY2028 EPS DKK 76.78, +0.9%, +0.6% and +0.1%. FY2027 revenue +1.1%, +0.7% and +0.8%; FY2027 EBITDA +0.7%, (1.2%) and (1.3%); FY2028 EBITDA +1.0%, (0.9%) and (1.1%).
  • Since 3-Nov: FY2027 EPS +0.1% (DKK 70.33 to 70.40); the 4 and 5-Nov readings are identical, so the post-statement round looks complete.
  • The trajectory: FY2028 EPS rose 0.9% over the month against 0.4% for FY2026, a slightly raised trajectory. Implied EPS growth is 8.0% (FY2026 to FY2027) and 9.1% (FY2027 to FY2028) on revenue growth of 4.6% and 3.6%, with the implied EBITDA margin rising from 21.0% to 21.3% and 21.7%: a synergy and insourcing case, not a volume case.
  • Why they rose: currency, as Bernstein, Deutsche Bank and Rothschild & Co Redburn stated (29 and 30-Oct; earnings review); Carlsberg raised its FY2026 translation tailwind to DKK 100m on 28-Oct.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your market note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Carlsberg has not yet passed it on its record: FY2027 EPS is down 0.2% over three months and 0.6% over six, and FY2027 and FY2028 EBITDA are down over both periods; only the last month is up, on currency. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 3-Nov expects cuts before raises: "revenue and composition cuts first (to April 2027), the share count second (from August 2027)".

The link: the SP has run far ahead of the estimates, as your note of 3-Nov says is usual ("Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"). Carlsberg's SP rose 18.2% over 20 sessions while FY2027 EPS rose 0.8% over the month, so the whole move is a re-rating. The memo's FY2027 EPS test (modal DKK 69 to 71, alarm below DKK 66) is met at DKK 70.40. Confirms the price lead: organic EPS raises from the sell-side before February. Refutes it: FY2027 EPS back below DKK 70 with the SP holding up, an SP running on rotation alone.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's SP has caught up with its rising average PT: the average PT rose 2.5% in a week, to DKK 1,069.9 on 4-Nov, but the SP rose faster, and the gap is now 3.7%, the narrowest against any month-end reading in 24 months. It was DKK 1,043.5 on 28-Oct (gap 6.4% on 3-Nov). Hypothesis: the average PT acts as a ceiling for Carlsberg. For it: the narrowest earlier month-end gap, 5.8% (May 2025), was followed by a fall of up to 19.9% within 63 sessions, and the gaps of 10.4% (February 2026) and 10.7% (July 2026) by falls of 17.5% and 12.1% to the next lows. Against it: PTs are now rising, unlike through October. Test: whether further PT raises follow the SP above DKK 1,070, or the SP stalls there.

The latest PT changes (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026, and the earnings review):

  • Raised: Deutsche Bank, Buy, DKK 1,160 to DKK 1,200 (29-Oct), calling the 3% post-statement fall "a buying opportunity"; Bernstein, Outperform, DKK 1,535 to DKK 1,550 (30-Oct), on a 13% EPS compound growth rate over the four years to FY2029.
  • Held: Morgan Stanley, Overweight, DKK 1,075 (4.2% above the 5-Nov close); Rothschild & Co Redburn, Buy, DKK 1,100; Jefferies, Hold, DKK 950 (all 29-Oct).
  • Now below the SP: Barclays DKK 930 (Equal Weight, 12-Oct), Jefferies DKK 950, HSBC DKK 980 (Hold) and DNB Carnegie DKK 995 (raised from DKK 990 on 15-Oct, earnings review); Carlsberg's SP is 3.7% to 10.9% above them.

Ratings: unchanged at 20 Buys, 8 Holds, no Sells since the 31-Oct month-end (21, 5 and 1 at 31-Aug). The Buy share has fallen from 78% to 71% while the SP rose: as from September 2024 to June 2025, Carlsberg's ratings move against the SP (they follow valuation) while its PTs move with it (they follow momentum).

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure and still widening. On the 4 and 5-Nov count: EPS +19% (12 raising, 7 cutting, of 26), EBITDA +59% (14 raising, 1 cutting, of 22), revenue +60% (16 raising, 1 cutting, of 25), PT +32% (12 raising, 4 cutting, of 25). On 3-Nov they were +15%, +55%, +56% and +28%; at 30-Sep (32%), (29%), (17%) and (22%). Where they diverge: EPS Net Upgrade Breadth is the weakest, with 7 analysts still cutting EPS while only 1 cuts revenue or EBITDA: plausibly cuts below the operating line (finance costs, minorities, the hybrid coupon, which the memo names as the lines brokers disagree on), though the sources read do not say.

Where analysts are cutting: Rothschild & Co Redburn cut its FY2027 organic revenue growth by 50 basis points to 3.0% (29-Oct) on "persistent consumer weakness in China that is likely to continue through the first half of 2027", and projects China's operating margin down 180 basis points to 22% in 2026; Bernstein trimmed FY2026 EPS 0.1% (30-Oct). Jefferies has kept Hold at DKK 950 since its 13-Sep downgrade on China. The other EPS cutters are not named in the sources read.

9. Peers

The whole of Beverages is at or near 52W highs, so Carlsberg's breakout is a group breakout; Carlsberg's own edge came on 30-Oct and 2-Nov, and in the two sessions since the previous run it lagged Heineken, AB InBev and Diageo.

  • SP technicals: on 5-Nov Heineken (€84.02), Campari, Rémy Cointreau and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners closed at 52W highs with Carlsberg; AB InBev closed 0.2% below its 22-Oct high and Diageo 0.1% below its 24-Feb high. Royal Unibrew is 25.9% below its 23-Feb high and still 2.0% below its absolute 200D MA.
  • Who led: from the 18-Sep low Carlsberg is up 22.7%, Heineken 18.8%, Diageo 16.6%, Royal Unibrew 12.0%, AB InBev 11.9%, against 11.1% for Consumer staples.
  • Volume: on 5-Nov the peers broke out on heavier volume than Carlsberg (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)): the confirming sign Carlsberg's own tape lacks.
  • Who moves with Carlsberg: Royal Unibrew (average correlation 0.73) and Heineken (0.72) track its daily moves most closely; on China news it trades with AB InBev (22 to 29-Oct).

Sell-side estimates (peer table): over three months Carlsberg's EPS revision is 0% and its revenue revision +1%, against (1%) and 0% for AB InBev and Heineken and (2%) and 0% for Royal Unibrew; only Diageo (+6%) and Rémy Cointreau (+5%) show real EPS raises in Beverages. Carlsberg's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +60% is the best in Beverages (Rémy Cointreau +53%, Campari +48%, Heineken +15%, AB InBev (20%)). For Carlsberg: the group is rising on price with flat estimates, as Carlsberg is.

Newsflow and guidance (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026, and the sources named):

  • Heineken beat on volume in its third quarter (organic +3.9% against 1.8% consensus, Asia Pacific +15.1%) and kept FY2026 organic operating profit guidance of 2% to 6% (Bernstein, 29-Oct).
  • Coca-Cola Europacific Partners beat on its third quarter on 3-Nov (currency-neutral sales +5.8% against 5.0%, guidance reiterated; Deutsche Bank, 3-Nov): positive for Carlsberg's soft drinks in 2026.
  • Coca-Cola HBC fell 17.9% on 5-Nov on 3.89x its 50D average volume, after a Russian businessman, Vadim Smagin, asked President Putin to place its Russian subsidiaries Multon and Multon Partners under temporary state management (Dnews, 6-Nov). Coca-Cola HBC said it had no notification from the Russian authorities (statement, 5-Nov, Reuters via TradingView). For Carlsberg: the Russian seizure risk crystallised in 2023 (the memo's "Russia's 2023 precedent"), so the read-across is to the sector's RS, which lost 2 points that day, not to Carlsberg's business; Carlsberg rose 0.5%.

What the peers imply for Carlsberg. The group sets Beverages' Sector RS, so a sell signal from Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 would come from a group-wide fall, as at the 31-Aug month-end. What would tell: Heineken and AB InBev holding their new highs supports Carlsberg's; heavy-volume down days across the brewers near these highs would be the group's first sign of distribution.

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • Most important: the breakout has followed through, but as part of a Beverages-wide breakout on the defensive rotation, on average volume of its own. Since 2-Nov the peers have matched or beaten Carlsberg on heavier volume, and its up/down volume over the advance (1.06) is thinner than its two successful advances of the past year.
  • Second: the SP has caught up with the sell-side. The average PT gap is 3.7%, the narrowest against any month-end reading in 24 months, and estimates are up under 1%, on currency, still down over three and six months: your upgrading rule of 3-Nov is not met on the record.
  • Third: the retest you are waiting for is far away: the longer MAs are 12.2% to 14.3% below the close, the rising 20D MA 7.3%.
  • The rules today: no sell signal from any of the six Sell Criteria; no tranche due for sale; all three tranches held.
  • The nearest sell test, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end): Carlsberg's Stock RS is 81, 11 points above the line of 70, after +11 over 1 week (from Fri 30-Oct), +27 over 4 weeks (Fri 9-Oct) and +19 over 13 weeks (Fri 7-Aug); Beverages' Sector RS is 80, 30 points above the line of 50, after +4, +26 and +22. At about 2.3 points of Stock RS per 1% of relative price (29-Oct to 5-Nov; a rank, so a guide only), about 5% of underperformance takes Stock RS below 70: at a flat market, an SP back near the DKK 981.8 ceiling.
  • How the tests interact: a group-wide fall can meet both halves at once, as from the 31-Jul month-end (Stock RS 68, Sector RS 68) to 31-Aug (49 and 47); a Carlsberg-only fall meets only the Stock RS half. Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high needs DKK 810.9 at a flat market (21.4% below); a tranche's 30% fall needs DKK 722.05 (30.0% below). A whole-holding sell signal sells Carlsberg when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link.
  • The record: this test gave its sell signals after Carlsberg's last two tops late, near the lows: at the 31-Mar month-end at DKK 803.2 (18.2% below the 12-Feb high) and at 31-Aug at DKK 883.4 (8.4% below the 30-Jul high).
  • The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov (today): Industry RS below 30 (Consumer staples 73) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (+0.72%); neither can plausibly give a sell signal.
  • Adds: the rules allow an add: no sell signal, and 2.04% of room below the 6.68% limit on Carlsberg's current 4.64% of capital (3.98% at entry): one 1.67% tranche.
  • Where Carlsberg stands for an add: none of the three stages. It is not pulling back or basing, not retesting a rising MA and not in a volatility contraction pattern: it closed at a 52W high, 7.9% above its 20D MA. The Master Dashboard's tests point to pulling back or basing (basing plausible, 3 of 4; pulling back possible, 2 of 4; healthy retest possible, 3 of 6; volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4); my reading: only a pullback larger than the usual weekly move (3.3%) puts Carlsberg there. No add trigger is present.
  • For your ruling: on 2-Nov you wrote "SUMMARY - Add 2-4% in 2 tranches over N2W". The room takes one 1.67% tranche and a second of at most 0.37%; within two weeks of 2-Nov, the rising 20D MA (DKK 955.74) is the only MA a pullback can realistically retest.
  • The dates: FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027 (confirmed); the memo names a possible Indian listing (market chatter of a December launch) as the one event that could bring the buyback forward. What would change this reading: the volume tests at the end of item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).

Richard decides.

12. Adding capital to Carlsberg: the rules and your add triggers (readings of 2026-11-05)

  • The rules: no sell signal from the six Sell Criteria {green}
    • Where it stands: The rules allow an add: none of Carlsberg's six Sell Criteria gives a sell signal.
    • Why: The nearest, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70: Beverages' Sector RS is 30 points above 50, Carlsberg's Stock RS 11 above 70.
    • What would change it: Carlsberg's Stock RS below 70 with Beverages' Sector RS below 50 at Mon 30-Nov.
  • The rules: room below the holding's limit {green}

The later years are cut fastest, so analysts are trimming the trajectory the case rests on. FY2028 EBITDA has fallen 4.0% over 3 months against FY2026's 0.7%. Consensus still has EBITDA growing 28.3% in FY2027 and 21.5% in FY2028 on revenue growth of 8.1% and 7.3%, so the implied EBITDA margin climbs from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% and 16.7%. The SP led and the estimates have not followed, which is the pattern your 3-Nov note describes ("Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"); the test is whether they follow within your 3-to-6-month window.

The reading is double-digit but not 20%, and below the investment memo's (3-Nov) view-changer of "the scanner rate holding above 15% into the holiday prints". FactSet consensus needs about 20% United States revenue growth in the second half of 2026 (United States sales bridge research, 4-Nov), and scanner data cover only about a third of United States sales (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 15-Apr-26).

11. Fevertree against each Sell Criterion (readings of 2026-11-05)
